현재 구현 기준으로, Ontology Atlas의 ACP runtime capability는 **“사용자의 코딩 에이전트를 앱 안에서 ACP v1로 실행하고, 현재 볼트의 MCP와 안전한 쓰기 관문을 붙이는 기능”**입니다. 온톨로지 스키마나 MCP 자체를 ACP가 소유하는 것은 아닙니다. [ACP capability 정의](/Users/stark/orca/workspaces/oh-my-ontology/main-2/docs/ontology/capabilities/acp-runtime.md:17)

먼저 문서 드리프트가 하나 있습니다. 온톨로지 문서에는 아직 “현재 Claude만”이라고 적혀 있지만, 최신 구현과 2026-08-24 결정에서는 Codex도 검증을 통과해 in-app chat 대상에 들어갔습니다. 따라서 아래는 최신 코드·결정 원장 기준입니다. [CHAT_ELIGIBLE]( /Users/stark/orca/workspaces/oh-my-ontology/main-2/src-tauri/src/acp.rs:170) · [Codex 복귀 결정]( /Users/stark/orca/workspaces/oh-my-ontology/main-2/docs/DECISIONS.md:20201)

| 경계 | 포함하는 것 | 제외하는 것 | 그렇게 한 이유 |
|---|---|---|---|
| 실행기 | 설치 상태 탐지, ACP 프로세스 실행, 앱 전용 설정 격리, 세션 종료 | 등록된 모든 에이전트를 곧바로 앱 채팅 대상으로 취급하지 않음 | 앱이 실제로 권한 관문을 검증한 실행기만 안전하다고 말할 수 있음. 현재는 `claude-acp`, `codex-acp`만 해당 |
| Atlas 연결 | `session/new`에 현재 볼트의 `atlas-vault` MCP 서버를 주입하고, 같은 온톨로지 도구를 사용 | ACP가 MCP를 대체하거나 별도의 온톨로지 API를 만듦 | Atlas의 고유 자산은 ACP가 아니라 로컬 Markdown 볼트와 MCP 도구이기 때문입니다. ACP는 그 위에 놓이는 실행 경로입니다. [vault MCP 연결]( /Users/stark/orca/workspaces/oh-my-ontology/main-2/src/features/acp-session/model/vault-mcp-server.ts:3) |
| 읽기/쓰기 | Atlas 읽기 도구는 볼트 안에서 자동 진행. 예: `get_concept`, `find_path`, `query_ontology`, `validate_vault`, `index_project` 등 | Atlas 쓰기 도구는 모두 변경 검토로 보냄. 새 도구가 읽기 목록에 없으면 자동으로 write 취급 | 의미가 바뀌는 작업은 사람의 판단과 provenance가 필요하기 때문입니다. [읽기 목록과 fail-closed 정책]( /Users/stark/orca/workspaces/oh-my-ontology/main-2/src/features/acp-session/model/atlas-tool-policy.ts:1) |
| 쓰기 승인 | `allow_once` 또는 `reject_once`, typed ontology change review, Codex의 경우 서버 측 MCP consent | semantic write의 `allow_always`, 무조건 자동 승인, 승인 불능 클라이언트에서의 쓰기 | Codex `read-only`가 직접 파일은 막아도 MCP 쓰기는 통과시킨 실측이 있었으므로, 쓰기를 수행하는 MCP 서버 자체에 관문을 둡니다. 관문을 표시할 수 없으면 fail-closed로 거절합니다. [MCP write consent]( /Users/stark/orca/workspaces/oh-my-ontology/main-2/mcp/src/write-consent.mjs:1) |
| 파일 범위 | 세션의 작업 폴더는 검증된 볼트. 볼트 내부는 자동 허용될 수 있고, 외부·미확인 경로는 질문 | `/`, 홈 디렉터리 자체, OS/app bundle, 외부 경로의 자동 쓰기, 세션 중 바뀐 symlink 경계 | 잘못 선택한 폴더에서 에이전트가 시스템 파일을 건드리는 사고를 막고, 경로 문자열이 아니라 정규화된 실제 경로를 기준으로 판단하기 위해서입니다. [경로 판정]( /Users/stark/orca/workspaces/oh-my-ontology/main-2/src-tauri/src/acp.rs:1408) |
| 작업 모드 | 안전성이 측정된 모드와, 아직 검증되지 않았음을 표시한 모드 | `bypasspermissions`, `agent-full-access`, Codex의 `agent` 등 권한 관문을 제거한다고 측정된 모드 | 모르는 모드를 안전하다고 간주하지 않기 위해서입니다. 단, 새롭지만 실제로 안전할 수도 있는 모드를 숨기는 것도 부정확하므로 “unverified”로 노출합니다. [mode safety]( /Users/stark/orca/workspaces/oh-my-ontology/main-2/src/features/acp-session/model/mode-safety.ts:19) |
| 프로토콜 범위 | ACP v1의 세션 초기화·prompt·update·permission request·세션 수명 관리 | ACP client가 file read/write, terminal, elicitation capability를 직접 선언하는 것 | ACP capability 선언만으로 실제 CLI의 도구 권한이 통제되지 않는다는 실측이 있었기 때문입니다. 실제 관문은 런타임 설정과 MCP 서버에 둡니다. [ACP client 경계]( /Users/stark/orca/workspaces/oh-my-ontology/main-2/src/features/acp-session/model/acp-client.ts:7) |
| 데이터·제품 경계 | 로컬 stdio MCP, 사용자가 이미 보유한 Claude/Codex 구독 인증, 앱 전용 설정 디렉터리 | Atlas API key, Atlas backend/login, Atlas가 provider 통신을 기록한다고 주장하는 것, 웹에서의 프로세스 실행 | 볼트는 로컬 Markdown/Git의 source of truth이고, 선택한 에이전트가 자신의 provider로 보내는 prompt·context·tool result는 별도 provider 경계입니다. [신뢰 경계]( /Users/stark/orca/workspaces/oh-my-ontology/main-2/docs/guide/trust.md:5) |

소유권도 분리되어 있습니다.

- `agent-integration`은 실행기 탐지·실행·MCP 연결을 담당합니다.
- MCP capability는 실제 온톨로지 읽기/쓰기 도구를 담당합니다.
- `graph-modeling`은 스키마와 typed ontology change review를 담당합니다.
- ACP는 이들을 새로 복제하지 않고 연결합니다. [도메인 포함/제외]( /Users/stark/orca/workspaces/oh-my-ontology/main-2/docs/ontology/domains/agent-integration.md:23)

즉, 이 capability의 핵심 경계는 **“에이전트를 앱 안으로 들이는 것”은 포함하지만, “에이전트에게 Atlas와 컴퓨터 전체에 대한 무제한 권한을 주는 것”은 제외한다**는 데 있습니다.

./src/entities/docs-vault/data/gateway-changelog.json:2:  "body": "# CHANGELOG\n\n> Major change history. Code commit messages answer *why*; this file answers *when / which surface changed*. Focused on **user-visible changes**, not PR-level granularity.\n>\n> Newest at the top. Entries stay date-based; the app version reached v1.0.0 on\n> 2026-07-27, so a release tag now carries the semver promise instead.\n\n---\n\n## 2026-08-25 · The released app carries its third-party notices\n\n- The macOS app and Windows installer now ship `NOTICE.md` and `LICENSE` inside the bundle. The app\n  statically links JavaScriptCore and WebKit through its compiled MCP sidecar, and ships the\n  Pretendard font, and both carry notice obligations that were not being met. Ontology Atlas itself\n  remains MIT; those licenses bind the component, not the program linking it.\n- The notice is generated from the actual dependency trees by `pnpm notice:build`, and\n  `pnpm notice:check` blocks a release whose notice has gone stale.\n\n## 2026-08-25 · A domain says when its description has fallen behind what it holds\n\n- A `domain` or `project` body is a summary of the nodes it contains. When that list changes and\n  nobody re-writes the description, the vault kept saying the old thing and nothing noticed:\n  every existing check asked whether the graph was intact, never whether it still told the truth.\n- `validate_vault` now reports `summaryFreshness`, and `query_ontology({operation:'maintenance_plan'})`\n  now raises a `rejudge_summary_membership` action, when a node's containment list changed after its\n  prose was last written. Both are advisory: nothing is blocked, no model is called, and no rewrite\n  is proposed. The body is a human judgement, so the vault asks for a judgement.\n- Outside a Git repository both report `checked: false` instead of a clean bill, because not looking\n  is not the same as finding nothing.\n\n## 2026-08-24 · Codex in-app chat pauses until Atlas MCP writes have an app-owned review gate\n\n- Installed-app acceptance proved that Codex `read-only` blocks direct file writes but can still\n  execute a self-registered Atlas MCP write without emitting the permission request the review card\n  depends on. The app no longer offers Codex in-app chat while that gap exists.\n- Codex remains detected and its external MCP connection/config path is unchanged. Claude Agent,\n  whose isolated session produces the app-owned permission checkpoint, remains available in chat.\n- Agent setup copy now scopes the no-port/no-network claim to the Atlas MCP child process and says\n  plainly that the coding agent itself may communicate with its provider.\n\n## 2026-08-23 · Selecting a node in the 3D dome lights its line to the top\n\n- In the dome view, pressing a node now lights the whole chain it hangs from (element to\n  capability to domain to the project at the apex) in the same indigo the selection already\n  uses, while the dome turns to face it. Pressing empty space lets it go. The flat map is\n  unchanged.\n\n## 2026-08-23 · The download page moves where the product is working\n\n- The evidence section now runs a short linked demo once it settles: three beats light a line of\n  the file on the right while the map on the left focuses the thing that line is (the node, its\n  domain, its dependency), then let go. Touching the map stops the demo immediately, and readers\n  with motion turned off simply get the resting section.\n- The agent scene's tool call arrives as typing, like a terminal writing, instead of fading in.\n  The text is still the measured session, unchanged.\n- The concept and relation counts under the map, and the file count in the evidence panel, count\n  up briefly when they first come into view.\n\n## 2026-08-23 · The gateway map labels only what the overview needs\n\n- The download page's evidence map no longer crams a label onto every name that fits. It labels\n  the hub, the seven domains, and the busiest capabilities (twelve names instead of\n  thirty-three), and every other concept stays as a dot until you hover or press it. All 82 dots\n  are still drawn, so the caption's count remains the picture's count.\n\n## 2026-08-23 · The demo clip loops without a control bar, and two sections read more plainly\n\n- The demo clip plays on a loop with no timecode or progress rail. It still pauses whenever the\n  section is off screen, and the play button remains for readers who turn motion off.\n- The evidence panel now shows only the lines of the file that carry meaning, and says how many it\n  left out. The four it drops are an id, a duplicate of the path above it, an empty list, and an\n  internal bookkeeping field.\n- The agents section says what the reader gets instead of naming a protocol: the app starts the\n  agent you already have, with no new key and no sign-in, and you approve a change before any file\n  moves.\n\n## 2026-08-23 · Every concept in the dogfood vault now has a Korean name\n\n- All 83 nodes carry a `display_ko`, up from 21. On a Korean screen the map, the\n  INDEX panel, node details, and the download page's evidence section no longer mix\n  English names into Korean text. Measured on `/ko/download/`: 33 labels drawn, none\n  Latin-only, where most were English before.\n- English screens are unchanged: they read `display_en` or the title as before.\n\n## 2026-08-23 · The evidence section shows one real file instead of counts\n\n- The right half of the download page's evidence section is now **one file that\n  actually exists in this repository**, together with what an agent reads out of\n  it. The kind counts and the sampled relation list are gone. A link opens that\n  file on GitHub so the claim can be checked.\n- None of those values are hand-typed: they are derived from the vault, and a\n  check fails when the committed copy and the vault disagree.\n- The section heading changed from claiming the map *is* this repository to naming\n  what actually happens: a few lines at the top of a file become the map.\n- Removed the caption under the agents section about agent writes ending up as a\n  line in a file.\n\n## 2026-08-23 · 시연 절이 가운데로 서고, 설명 문구가 실제 영상을 말합니다\n\n- 시연 절의 제목·설명·영상·캡션이 **한 축에 가운데로** 섭니다. 영상만 가운데이고\n  제목은 왼쪽이라 오른쪽이 비어 보이던 것을 바로잡았습니다.\n- **설명 문구가 없어진 장면을 말하고 있었습니다.** 「폴더를 고르면 지도가 되고,\n  에이전트가 그 지도를 읽고 씁니다」라고 적혀 있었는데, 지금 9초 클립에는 폴더를\n  고르는 장면도 에이전트도 없습니다. 실제로 보이는 것을 그대로 적었습니다.\n- 「무편집 · 무음」, 「한 테이크」처럼 만든 사람만 아는 말을 걷어내고 평범한 말로\n  바꿨습니다.\n\n## 2026-08-23 · 관문의 제목이 한 글자씩 쳐지고, 시연 무대가 자기 제목과 다시 맞습니다\n\n- 다운로드 페이지 제목이 커서와 함께 **한 글자씩 쳐집니다**. 한국어 1.14초, 영어\n  1.80초에 끝나며, 문장이 길수록 글자당 시간이 짧아져 어느 언어도 더 오래 기다리지\n  않습니다. 모션을 줄여 둔 사용자에게는 처음부터 문장이 다 보입니다.\n- 글자를 덧붙이는 방식이 아니라 자리를 미리 잡아 두고 잉크만 켜는 방식이라, 치는\n  동안 아래 내용이 한 픽셀도 밀리지 않습니다.\n- 시연 영상이 자기 절 제목과 **369px 어긋나 있던 것**을 바로잡았습니다. 세 절 중\n  이 절만 가운데 정렬이라 스크롤할 때 기둥이 끊겨 보였습니다. 영상 크기는 그대로입니다.\n\n## 2026-08-22 · 시연 영상을 영어로도 따로 찍었습니다\n\n- 영어 페이지의 시연 영상이 이제 **영어 화면 녹화**입니다. 화면 글자도 노드\n  이름도 영어이고, 한국어 페이지는 한국어 녹화를 그대로 봅니다. 「두 언어가 같은\n  녹화를 공유합니다」라는 안내 문구는 사실이 아니게 되어 지웠습니다.\n- 두 자산이 실수로 다시 한 파일이 되는 것을 막는 검사를 걸었습니다. 처음 이틀\n  동안 실제로 그 상태였는데 기존 검사는 전부 초록이었습니다. 둘 다 존재했고,\n  둘 다 선언한 길이였기 때문입니다.\n\n## 2026-08-22 · 관문의 시연 영상이 9초 한 장면으로 바뀌고, 한국어 화면에서 다시 재생됩니다\n\n- 시연 영상을 3분짜리 기능 투어에서 **9초 한 장면**으로 바꿨습니다. 이미 연결된\n  폴더에서 지도가 그려지고, 노드 하나를 누르면 이웃만 남으면서 그 아래·위·근거\n  문서가 함께 뜹니다. 폴더를 고르는 장면은 담지 않습니다. 컷도 배속도 없어\n  조립과 클릭이 실제 기계에서 걸리는 만큼 걸립니다. 자산도 12.3MB 에서 1.5MB 로\n  줄었습니다.\n- **한국어 페이지에서 시연 영상이 아예 재생되지 않던 문제를 고쳤습니다.** 영상\n  요소가 언어에 따라 다시 태어나는데 화면 진입을 감시하던 쪽이 버려진 요소를\n  계속 보고 있어서, 영어 페이지는 자동 재생되고 한국어 페이지만 포스터에 멈춰\n  있었습니다(2026-08-20 이후). 실측: `/en/` 2.97초 진행 · `/ko/` 0초 정지.\n\n## 2026-08-22 · 에이전트가 찾은 곳을 지도가 바로 보여 주고, 큰 지도는 한 번에 펼칠 수 있습니다\n\n- 지도 옆 에이전트가 Atlas의 `get_concept`를 호출하면 그 노드가 바로 선택되고,\n  `find_path`를 호출하면 정확한 최단 경로 노드와 관계선만 밝아집니다. 답변 문장을\n  추측해서 움직이지 않고 현재 볼트에 실제로 있는 typed tool input만 따릅니다.\n  Claude ACP가 입력을 뒤늦은 `tool_call_update`로 완성하는 실제 순서도 합쳐 읽습니다.\n- 상단에 「전체 펼치기」를 추가했습니다. 접혀 있던 containment 묶음을 모두 열고\n  전체 노드를 자동으로 화면에 맞춥니다. 다시 누르면 전체 접기로 돌아가며, 개별\n  `+N` 전개와 공유 링크의 `?open=` 동작은 그대로입니다.\n- 노드가 많은 지도를 여는 동안에는 큰 소개 문단 대신 화면 중앙의 회로형 스피너와\n  지금 하는 일 한 문장만 보입니다. INDEX 루트가 늦게 도착해도 내부가 기본으로\n  열리며, 사용자가 직접 닫은 루트는 존중합니다.\n- 노드 상세의 7개 액션 타일을 주 행동 하나와 「편집」·「더 보기」 메뉴로\n  정리했습니다. 중복 연결 합계, INDEX 하단의 에이전트 인계 푸터, 상시 관계선\n  범례, LNB 하단의 중복 활동 아이콘도 걷었습니다. 관계선 표본은 `?` 도움말에서\n  필요할 때 확인합니다.\n- 1024px에서 전체 펼치기 도구줄이 INDEX와 노드 상세을 침범하던 문제를 실측해,\n  1280px 아래에서는 오른쪽 두 번째 줄로 내리거나 상세이 열릴 때 숨깁니다.\n- 14인치에서 에이전트 패널과 노드 상세이 함께 열리면 상단 도구줄이 상세 폭을\n  제외한 남은 지도 중앙으로 자동 이동합니다. 에이전트 패널이 열린 동안 중앙·우측\n  버튼은 아이콘 밀도로 접혀 서로 겹쳐 보이지 않습니다.\n- 에이전트 패널이 지도를 줄이는 매 프레임에 전체·선택·영역·경로 중 지금 보던\n  상태의 카메라도 같은 클럭으로 새 가용 폭을 따라갑니다. 도크가 멈춘 뒤 지도가\n  마지막 목표에 속도 0으로 착지해 underdamped 되튕김으로 한 번 더 움직이지\n  않으며, 노드 상세을 닫을 때도 퇴장 중인 패널 폭을 최종 화면에 남기지 않아\n  전체 그래프가 한쪽으로 치우치지 않습니다.\n- 오른쪽 대화창은 창 높이를 통째로 덮는 벽 대신 위·아래·오른쪽을 12px 띄운\n  인셋 패널로 보입니다. 두 에이전트 갈래 모두 기존 지도 패널의 네 면 테두리,\n  radius, 표면색, 그림자를 공유하며 지도 폭을 양보하는 도크 동작은 그대로입니다.\n  패널 왼쪽 지도 컨트롤도 같은 seam에서 12px만 물러나 둘 사이 간격을 24px로\n  줄이고, 패널과 같은 260ms 동안 따라갑니다.\n- 대화창을 열 때 빈 검은 패널부터 보이던 순서를 바로잡았습니다. 헤더·폴더 안내·\n  추천·작성 칸은 도크 첫 프레임부터 최종 자리에 있고, 상태 배지의 작은 스피너와\n  「연결 중 → 준비됨」만 바뀝니다. ACP 프로세스 시작은 폭 전환 뒤에 이어져 지도\n  리플로우를 끊지 않습니다. 카메라가 마지막 픽셀에 도착할 240ms도 먼저 보장해\n  프로세스 부팅 뒤 지도가 다시 움직이지 않으며, 큰 패널에는 추가 scale 모션을\n  겹치지 않습니다.\n- 작업 상태 행 안에 붙어 있던 알림 종과 숫자를 분리했습니다. 종은 우상단 지도\n  도구줄의 맨 오른쪽 정사각 아이콘 하나로 서고, 숫자는 버튼을 늘리지 않는 겹침\n  배지로 보입니다. 상태를 누르면 현재 작업만, 종을 누르면 알림·작업 영수증만\n  열립니다. 알림함은 기존 280px 대신 지도 패널의 352px 반응형 폭을 씁니다.\n- 분리한 작업 상태 행이 짧은 도구줄의 폭을 상속해 `Codex · 마지막 작업…`부터\n  잘리던 문제를 고쳤습니다. 내용의 자연 폭으로 서되 화면 안에서 최대 520px로\n  제한해 에이전트 이름과 시각은 먼저 온전히 읽힙니다.\n- 「에이전트」 목적지의 「이 도구로 대화 열기」는 이제 선택한 실행기를 일회성\n  요청으로 남기고 지도로 이동한 뒤, 그 실행기가 실제 준비된 것을 확인하고 대화\n  도크를 엽니다. 지도 구독자가 없는 `/agents`에서 window 이벤트만 보내 아무 일도\n  없던 경로를 없앴고, 버튼도 한 단계 큰 32px급 컨트롤로 바꿨습니다.\n- 앱 업데이트는 prerelease를 제외하는 GitHub의 `/releases/latest` 대신 Pages의\n  안정된 manifest 주소를 사용합니다. 설정 안 검사 실패와 설치 실패를 구분하고,\n  같은 실패를 원시 영문 토스트로 한 번 더 보여 주지 않습니다.\n\n## 2026-08-22 · 에이전트 묶음 변경을 전부 보고, 결정 결과를 다시 찾을 수 있습니다\n\n- `add_concepts`·`add_relations` 묶음 요청은 이제 “N건”과 첫 항목만 보여 주지\n  않습니다. 모든 항목이 accordion 행으로 보이고, 고른 관계 한 건의 방향과 이유가\n  지도 점선 미리보기와 함께 바뀝니다. 허용·거절은 묶음 전체라는 사실도 카드가\n  먼저 밝힙니다.\n- 앱 안에서 허용하거나 거절한 ontology 쓰기는\n  `.ontology-atlas/acp-work.jsonl`에 로컬 작업 영수증으로 남습니다. 활동 팝오버에서\n  요청·에이전트·도구·결정·최종 상태·변경 대상을 다시 펼칠 수 있습니다. 대화 전문,\n  생각, 도구 출력, 절대 경로는 저장하지 않습니다.\n- starter vault는 코드 폴더가 없을 때 에이전트에게 vault 자체를 소스처럼 훑게 하지\n  않습니다. 먼저 project의 코드 폴더 연결로 보내고, 연결을 확인한 뒤에만\n  근거 제안 → 사람 검토 → 승인된 batch 쓰기 → validate/compile 순서의 구축 prompt를\n  제공합니다.\n- 에이전트 답변의 Markdown 표에 헤더 면, 행 구분선, 셀 여백을 적용했습니다.\n  377px 도크에서도 표가 패널을 밀어내지 않고 자기 안에서 가로 스크롤합니다.\n\n## 2026-08-22 · 지도에서 에이전트가 지금 무엇을 하는지 바로 읽을 수 있습니다\n\n상단의 `codex-mcp-client 작업 중`은 내부 연결 이름과 추측한 상태를 섞었고, 옆의\n파란 개념 이름은 현재 대상인지 마지막 기록인지 설명하지 않았습니다. 이제 화면은\n감사 로그 원문을 보존하면서 사람이 아는 제품명과 확인된 작업 상태를 분리합니다.\n\n- `codex-mcp-client`와 `codex-acp`는 화면에서 `Codex`로 보입니다. Claude, Cursor,\n  다른 에이전트도 알려진 제품명으로 표시하고, 모르는 이름은 지어내지 않습니다.\n- fresh heartbeat가 있을 때만 `계획 중`, `편집 중`, `검증 중`, `승인 기다림`을\n  현재형으로 말합니다. 최근 쓰기만 관측했으면 `변경 감지`, 끝났으면\n  `마지막 작업`으로 구분해 계속 일하는 척하지 않습니다.\n- 상태 줄을 누르면 현재 요청, 단계, 지도 대상, 다음 행동, 마지막 도구가 한 카드에\n  보입니다. 도구 호출 알림을 쏟지 않고, 기존 작업 시작·끝/구조 변화 요약은\n  「최근 기록」으로 분리해 유지합니다. 카드는 오른쪽 지도 도구 열과 겹치지 않아\n  뒤의 아이콘이 작업 행 액션처럼 비치지 않습니다.\n- 파란 대상 앞에 `현재 대상:` 또는 `마지막 변경:`을 붙였습니다. 누르면 페이지를\n  다시 띄우지 않고 현재 지도에서 해당 노드를 바로 선택합니다. 그래서 기존 볼트가\n  잠깐 쇼핑몰 샘플로 바뀌었다 돌아오는 flash도 없습니다.\n- 인앱 ACP는 사용자 요청과 실제 tool/permission 상태를 heartbeat로 갱신합니다.\n  도구 입력이 현재 볼트의 실재 노드를 가리킬 때만 지도에 agent focus ring이 뜹니다.\n- 눌러도 작동하지 않던 `사람이 쓴 것` INDEX 탭과 사람 저작을 검토 대기로 오인한\n  붉은 링을 없앴습니다. `created_by` 데이터는 provenance로 그대로 남습니다.\n- reserved reader guide인 `아틀라스 자기 볼트`(`vault-readme`)는 문서함에 남지만,\n  더 이상 지도·INDEX·개념 수·관계 편집 대상으로 나타나지 않습니다.\n- 우측 에이전트 패널을 열면 INDEX는 저장된 기본값을 바꾸지 않고 잠시 접혀,\n  대화하면서 판단할 지도 폭을 지킵니다. 패널을 닫으면 INDEX가 원래대로 돌아오고,\n  접힌 INDEX 탭을 직접 열면 에이전트 패널이 물러납니다.\n- 에이전트의 생각 조각과 도구 호출은 질문 한 차례마다 `작업 과정` 한 줄로\n  접힙니다. 답변은 별도 본문으로 남고, 상세이 필요할 때만 펼칠 수 있습니다.\n  상세 안의 `**`·백틱·목록도 문자 그대로 노출하지 않고 Markdown으로 읽힙니다.",

./docs/DECISIONS.md:2799:## 2026-08-18 (72) — 에이전트 절은 검증이 아니라 **앱 안 대화**를 판다: `mcp-verify` 터미널 → ACP 채팅 실측 재연
./docs/DECISIONS.md:2801:**소집**: 소유자 직접 지적 6건 라운드 (*"이건 뭔말인지를 모르겠어.. 내가 강조하고싶은건 서비스 안에 acp가 들어갔거든? 에이전트가 연동되어서 채팅만으로 온톨로지 분석이 가능하다는걸 강조하고싶은데"*) · **트리거**: 관문 절 하나가 말하는 내용의 교체(포지셔닝 카피)
./docs/DECISIONS.md:2802:**결정**: 관문 ④ 에이전트 절의 장면을 `mcp-verify` 터미널 재연에서 **앱 안 대화(ACP) 실측 재연**으로 바꾼다(`AcpChatScene`). 재연되는 왕복은 지어내지 않고 원장 2026-08-16 (7) 의 실측 그대로다 — 사용자의 한국어 문장 → `add_relation | "사용자 설명: …(2026-08-16)"` 도구 호출 원문(번역 금지) → 「관계 1건이 frontmatter 에 적혔다」. 카드 3장은 ① 이미 쓰는 에이전트 그대로(Claude Agent · Codex — 앱은 실행기만) ② 폴더 밖은 먼저 묻는다(권한 관문) ③ 남는 건 마크다운(`git diff`). 구 터미널이 팔던 「실왕복 검증」은 거짓이 아니라 **개발자용 장면**이었다 — 처음 온 사람에게 이 절이 할 말은 「에이전트가 앱 안에 살고, 채팅이 곧 분석」 하나다.
./docs/DECISIONS.md:2803:**적용 규칙(공개 가능 경계 — 원장 2026-08-16 (5) 를 그대로 계승)**: ① 라이선스 전부 통과(ACP 규격·양 어댑터·레지스트리·Codex Apache-2.0, 우리 MIT, 재배포 0 — 어댑터는 사용자 기기에서 npx) ② 우리 실행기 목록을 설명하는 문구에 "Claude Code" 금지 — 레지스트리 허용 이름(Claude Agent)만, 게이트는 `vendor-naming.contract.test.ts` + 이 절 전용 단언(`DownloadPage.test.tsx`) ③ 열린 약관 질문(claude.ai 구독 경유) 위에 서지 않는다 — 카피는 전부 「이미 쓰는 에이전트를 연결한다」로 쓰고, 우리가 모델 접근을 제공한다는 인상을 주는 문장 0(같은 단언이 잠근다). `$ATLAS` 자리 표시가 절에서 사라지므로 `cliPlaceholderHint` 동반 의무도 이 절에서는 소멸(계약이 자리 표시를 보여주는 파일만 본다).
./docs/DECISIONS.md:2807:**기록된 반대**: 「`mcp-verify` 터미널은 이 제품이 설정 파일이 아니라 실왕복을 검증한다는 차별점의 증거였다 — 지우면 그 논증을 잃는다」. 기각 이유: 그 검증은 여전히 제품에 있고(연결 시트 · CLI), 관문 절 하나가 낼 수 있는 생각은 하나뿐이다 — 소유자가 고른 하나는 ACP 다. 터미널 장면이 필요해지면 가이드 문서가 제자리다.
./docs/DECISIONS.md:2808:**반증 조건**: ACP 없는 환경(웹 전용 · 터미널 MCP 사용자)의 방문자가 이 절을 보고 「앱 없이는 에이전트를 못 붙인다」로 오독하는 관측이 나오면 — 절에 MCP(앱 밖 에이전트) 경로 한 줄을 되살린다. 약관 질문이 ③과 다르게 닫히면(원장 2026-08-16 (5) 재검토) 이 절의 카피도 같은 라운드에서 다시 본다.
./docs/DECISIONS.md:3093:**서명**: Codex — fresh-init RED·runtime 응답·공개 schema 대조 · 진안 — main 머지 마무리 요청
./docs/DECISIONS.md:3375:**결정**: ACP 런타임이 볼트 설정에서 같은 MCP 명령을 스스로 읽는 경우에도,

Thus, ACP is not a replacement for MCP servers but a path that sits on top of them. capabilities/reviewed-ontology-writing: "ACP permission requests reuse the reviewed ontology writing contract: read tools continue, while write tools pause on the shared typed change review before allow_once or reject_once." }
relates: [capabilities/reviewed-ontology-writing]
---

## Definition

The ability to detect ACP (Agent Client Protocol) v1 coding-agent executors already installed on the device, directly launch only those with a verified app-owned permission gate (currently Claude Agent), and apply scoped permission review. No new Atlas API key or vendor integration is needed: it uses the subscription authentication already held by the launched tool.


## Behaviour Contract

Ten things this capability actually does:

1. **Detection.** The executor status of this device is divided into five categories: `ready`, `cli-missing`, `node-missing`, `uvx-missing`, and `binary-missing`. The reason for not collapsing the status into just "installed/not installed" is that the user's required actions differ for each state.
2. **The list comes from a committed snapshot.** The ACP registry is fetched once at build time and saved to `src-tauri/src/acp-registry.json`; icons are also bundled into `public/acp-icons/`. The committed registry and icons require no runtime network lookup. Starting a selected coding agent is a separate boundary: that agent may communicate with its own provider.
3. **Configuration isolation.** Sessions launched by the app use its own configuration directory within the app data folder (`CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`). Inheriting the user's global settings would mean the app cannot block anything for users who have set "allow all" in their terminal. Credentials are not copied but pointed to via symbolic links.
4. **Permission judgment.** Atlas read tools inside the selected vault proceed automatically. Atlas write tools pause on the shared typed change review and continue only after `allow_once`; `reject_once` cancels them. Other requests are judged by scope: inside-vault paths may proceed, while outside or unresolved paths ask the user. The basis for path judgment is not the title string but the `toolCall.rawInput.file_path` (absolute path) from the original permission request. The vault root is verified and normalized by `acp_start` and bound to the native session rather than trusted from each screen request. If the session is missing or the root is invalid, the app asks. If the root path changes to an external symbolic link after session start, normalized-path mismatch rejects it so the boundary cannot shift during the session.
5. **Working directory hygiene.** The working directory of a session must pass the same vault root judgment as the folder picker. Filesystem roots, the home directory itself, and OS/app directories are rejected.
6. **Workflow safety state.** Modes provided by the adapter are categorized as verified, risky, or unverified. Verified modes are hidden by removing permission checks, while unverified modes are preserved in `AcpSessionChoices.unverifiedModeIds` to display "unverified" along with its meaning in the existing selector.
7. **Process tree termination.** Children have their own process group; termination sends SIGTERM and waits up to 1 second before sending SIGKILL to terminate the entire group. Completion is determined by the survival of the original PGID, not the leader PID, so grandchildren that ignore TERM are not missed even if the leader is reaped first. If grandchildren launched by the adapter are not terminated together, processes will remain after the app is closed.

8. **Map synchronization.** Compares the typed input of actually called Atlas `get_concept` and `find_path` in the current user turn against the current vault's slug table, then moves to the same HomePage node focus or exact shortest path lens. Natural language answers or non-existent slugs are not used as map movement grounds. The actual order in which the Claude adapter completes streamed input with status-less `tool_call_update.rawInput` after a pending `tool_call` is merged into the same tool action before determining intent.
9. **Inset conversation surface.** Maintains the outer flex dock that actually yields map width, but displays the ACP/API-key conversation surface visible to the user within the top/bottom/right spacing ramp. It shares INDEX/node inspector's existing panel radius, border, surface, and shadow so it doesn't look like a black wall covering the window height without boundaries. The map control rail on the left of the panel is also pulled to 12px on the opposite side of the seam, limiting the gap between them to 24px, and moves with the same clock/curve as dock reflow.
10. **First frame connection state.** Separates conversation scaffold from session booting; header, empty guidance, vault-derived suggestions, and composer are drawn from the first frame the dock opens. After the remaining 240ms landing of the camera live spring after width reflow, ACP start is allowed; in the meantime, only the spinner and `connecting` text of the same status badge change. For large panels, overlay fade is used without overlapping scale/translate chrome motion so that layout reflow becomes the primary action.

## Boundaries
- **The "Agents" destination (`/agents/`) and the conversation panel on the right of the home map are the current user surface.** Selecting a measured runtime for isolation gates opens `HomePage` which passes the current vault as working folder and MCP server to `AcpChatPanel`. "Open chat with this tool" at the destination puts the selected runtime id into a one-shot queue in sessionStorage, moves to the map, and is consumed only when HomePage confirms the same runtime's readiness. Paths that only send window events without subscribers in `/agents` are not used.

  ⚠️ This line was *"The 'Runtime' section of settings … is neither a separate path nor a new screen"* until 2026-08-20. The archive (90) overturned it. Installation/connection is an operational task with progress status, not just picking values, so a blocking modal didn't fit the vessel. The same component (`AcpRuntimeSettings`) stands at both the destination and settings sheet; whether to draw an intro line is decided by the caller.
- **Starter vault's project source comes first.** Without source binding, empty conversation recommendations open code folder connections from the project dataset instead of agent analysis, re-read sidecar transitions on the same screen, and only then provide the source-evidence-first build prompt.
- **The session's vault MCP server maintains only one set.** Redundant injection is skipped only when a measured runtime already reads the same valid server from its vault config. That Codex self-read path is retained for external setup evidence, but it does not grant in-app chat eligibility.
- **The app offers in-app chat only for a runtime with an app-owned permission gate, and today that is just `claude-acp`.** Others remain visible for detection and external connection setup. Codex `read-only` blocks direct file access, but an installed-app probe showed a self-registered Atlas MCP write could still run without a permission request, so Codex chat stays unavailable until the app owns that MCP checkpoint.
- `isolated` reports Rust configuration isolation only. In-app eligibility additionally requires a proven ontology-write checkpoint and outside-folder gate; detection, external MCP setup, configuration isolation, and chat eligibility are distinct states. Unmeasured work modes in an eligible conversation remain explicitly unverified.
- Permission verdict IPC receives only `sessionId` and request path. WebView has no argument to redeclare `vaultRoot` and change native session boundaries.
- Browsers cannot launch processes. If `isAcpBridgeAvailable()` is false, the screen explains why it doesn't work and where it does.
- Windows process tree ownership (Job Object) is outside this fragment. It attempts with `taskkill /T` and grandchildren may remain if it fails.

## Definition
Surfaces enabling AI coding agents (Claude Code, Codex, Cursor) and developers to read and write the same ontology vault as humans: MCP servers, terminal CLI, in-app connect flow, and the ACP executor layer that directly launches coding agents already installed by the user.

## Evidence
- README.md: "Your agent reads and maintains it over MCP... one button writes your agent's config and proves the connection."
- mcp/src/index.js · mcp/src/tool-inventory.mjs: coupled boundary deriving `tools/list` and
  full/read-only initialize inventory from active registry
- cli/src/commands/agent-brief.mjs: passing `--project` as explicit project selector matching MCP `agent_brief.project`

## Inclusion / Exclusion
- Included: MCP read/write tools (`mcp/`) advertising current list at runtime, local CLI,
  in-app connect button, client config writing and `mcp-verify` connection proof,
  ACP executor detection and launching with isolated settings within the app
  (`capabilities/acp-runtime`)
- Excluded: the graph schema itself (that belongs to the graph-modeling domain)

## Project Meaning Handover

`finalize_project_meaning` bundles the competency answer for human-readable project Markdown into body digest and graph/source provenance after graph write, vault validation, and project compile.
the sidecar does not store raw answers, raw witnesses, or private source root/remote.
Subsequently, the new MCP process's `agent_brief.meaningAssessment` re-validates against the current Markdown and inventory; if source currentness cannot be confirmed, it closes as `review_required` even if a stored receipt exists.

The CLI `agent-brief --project <slug>` is not an option to merge results from multiple projects, but a selector that explicitly specifies one project containment tree to read the same MCP `agent_brief`.

## Confidence
high (0.9): MCP registry and CLI integration test directly verify the current contract

## Definition

A single location (`/agents/`) on this computer where you **receive, install, attach, fix, and initiate conversations with** AI coding tools. It is the eighth destination of the rail.

## Why Destination (2026-08-20, Chapter 90)

Until 2026-08-20, this task occupied two cells in the settings sheet ("Executor"·"MCP Connection"), while a separate connection sheet on the map handled the same function. **Three addresses for one task**.

The rationale is not screen width (measured install app: 46–47% of the sheet remains empty on the normal path). The rationale is **container**:

- Modals block the background and own Esc. You cannot see the map while receiving 52MB.
- **Settings are where values are chosen**, and this is an **operational task with progress**.

This mirrors the separation of skills from documentation (2026-08-09): different questions asked imply different destinations.

## What This Section Contains

1. **Executor List**: Tools confirmed on this device are expanded first; the rest are collapsed.
2. **Connection Check**: Review eight steps and fix what can be fixed right here.
3. **App-Specific Install**: Download Node and CLI only within the app folder. Version pinning · hash
   verification · command source preview (Chapters 88·89). Progress and completion remain on screen, persisting even if the window is closed and reopened.
4. **MCP Connection**: Configuration that exposes this folder to external agents. Also available via web.
   MCP attaches to the **folder**, not the screen (2026-08-01 Chapter).

## Boundaries

- **API Keys and workspaces remain in settings.** The former is governed by the 2026-08-16 "Path Freeze · Non-highlight", while the latter involves a different axis of Vault's response (`local-vault-management`).
- **Draw the MCP configuration panel only after opening Vault** (owner confirmation 2026-08-21). Without Vault, there is no configuration to save; saying "cannot save" at that point refers to a file that does not yet exist.
- **Eight rails are the upper limit** (owner signature). In the minimum window (720px), the eighth rail leaves only 8px remaining and enters; on zoom level 1.1 with width >2400, it requires 761px, exceeding limits, so rail scrolling is enforced alongside the cap.
- Programs cannot be launched on the web. Explain the reason, where to go, and **what can still be done on this screen**.

## Rationale

- src/views/agents/ui/AgentsPage.tsx: Main destination body
- src/widgets/app-settings-menu/ui/AcpRuntimeSettings.tsx: Executor list · check
- src/widgets/app-settings-menu/ui/AgentSetupSection.tsx: MCP connection section (Vault gate)
- src/features/acp-doctor/ui/AgentDoctor.tsx: Eight checks · repairs · install progress
- src/features/acp-doctor/model/use-install-notice.ts: Install completion rail badge
- src/shared/config/destinations.ts: Destination listing (eight-rail cap contract)
- tests/contract/destination-shortcuts.contract.test.ts: Cap · scroll handling contract
- tests/e2e/web-surface-smoke.spec.ts: Web downgrade 3-point contract
- docs/DECISIONS.md 2026-08-20 (90)

## Confidence

high (0.9): The route, migration, and gate are aligned with the contract and end-to-end; I opened both screens directly in the installed app to verify. The scene where the rail badge actually appears in the installed app was substituted with unit tests and browser navigation persistence measurements. That spot still lacks real-world installation verification.

## Definition
A local-first ontology workbench that transforms frontmatter from a local Markdown vault into a computable graph with types (domain, capabilities, implementation evidence, dependencies, impacts). Developers use CLI/web, and AI coding agents use MCP to read and write the same files.

## Evidence
- README.md: "Your AI coding agent forgets your codebase between sessions... turns the Markdown in your repository into a graph of your product"
- AGENTS.md: Project overview ("a local-first ontology workbench for understanding a product/system from business core to implementation evidence")

## In Scope / Out of Scope
- In scope: Graph schema, compilation, querying, MCP/CLI agent integration, local vault management, topology browsing, project portfolio, onboarding/deployment/app shell
- Out of scope: Backend/authentication/cloud collaboration (permanently removed in R10, conditionally reintroduced only for Layer 2)

## Confidence
high (direct quote from README + AGENTS.md)

## Competency answers

### scope: answered

What product/system outcome and user problem define the ontology scope?

Ontology Atlas helps people and AI coding agents preserve and judge one shared, local-first meaning model of a product and the implementation evidence behind it.

- Evidence: `README.md`

19-
20-## 0. At a glance
21-
22:> **Mission v3**: "One product/system, one ontology, that people and their AI agents grow together."
23:> **Current framing**: an agent-native, human-sovereign meaning layer: typed enough for Claude Code, Cursor, and Codex; plain Markdown and Git diffs for human judgment.
24:> **Operating model**: single-user tool. Local-first vault. No login, no backend. **4 surfaces (desktop app · CLI · MCP · Website)** — daily heavy-lift ontology work happens in the installed app / CLI / MCP; on the hosted website, `/` is the gateway until a vault is loaded, then routes to the topology map, while `/topology` remains the explicit map address and `/download` carries the current release path.
25:> **Brand split**: **Ontology Atlas** is the user-facing desktop app / website brand and release asset identity. `ontology-atlas` remains the repo, CLI binary, and MCP package name.
26-
27-The product should not feel like an ontology editor. The core user-visible loop
28:is `init -> bootstrap -> MCP-backed agent answer -> agent sync proposal -> git
29:diff review -> better next agent task`.
30-
31-| Surface | Entry | Audience |
32-|---|---|---|
33:| **Desktop app** (macOS · Windows x64 beta) | signed/notarized macOS DMG or unsigned Windows beta NSIS → installed local workbench; first run opens `/docs/?intent=local` vault setup welcome; primary workbench routes `/topology`, `/docs`, `/ontology/insights`, `/projects`, `/agents`, and `/git` | daily visual ontology work — pick a local vault folder, edit markdown-backed nodes/relations, reopen recent vaults without visiting the hosted site |
34:| **CLI** (R12 / R14 / R15+ · 54 commands) | `init / agent-setup / agent-files / agent-activity / add / import / list / find / validate / mcp-verify / query / compile / export` (vault basics + existing-vault Claude/Codex config repair + read-only agent-file map/drift readout + explicit live activity heartbeat + installed MCP health/graph-query smoke + deterministic graph compile + standard-format interop export) · `index / analyze / infer-imports / bootstrap / preflight / snapshot` (autonomous ingest, project ontology indexing, commit preflight, and vault-scoped git snapshot commits) · `backlinks / orphans / path / explain / all-paths / reachability / relation-check / relate / rename / merge / delete` (graph CRUD + direct/path/common-neighbor explanation + bounded traversal + transitive closure + write preflight + write) · `match-nodes / match-edges / domain-matrix / facets / schema / pattern-walk / project-map / overview / hubs / blast-radius / cycles / components / topological-order / health / agent-brief / workspace-brief / growth / maintenance / node / similar` (graph deep dive — `query_ontology` ops, including graph DB-style node/edge scans, relation dashboard facets, relation schema patterns, explicit traversal and project maps, connected island checks, prerequisite ordering, relationship explanation, domain coupling matrix, agent handoff, and growth/maintenance queues) | developer terminal — vault scaffold, daily exploration, bulk import, MCP sanity check, live agent activity handoff, commit-time vault impact preview, graph deep dive (same authority as AI agent via MCP) |
35:| **MCP** (R5 / R7 / R11 / R14 / R16 / R17) | current runtime read/write inventory over JSON-RPC (`tools/list`; prove with `mcp-verify`) | AI agent (Claude Code, Codex, Cursor) — explicit vault/repo root proof · read for context · write back findings · vault-scoped Git status/local snapshots · safe relation removal/replacement and concept reclassification · bootstrap/index projects · finalize project competency receipts · compile/query/validator-backed health and fresh categorical meaning assessment |
36-| **Website** | GitHub Pages static export / `/` + `/topology` + `/download` | With no active vault, `/` is the gateway; with a loaded local vault it is the topology map, as is explicit `/topology`. `/download` is the product intro + current release download path. Only `/docs`'s own separate local-source *browsing* tab stays desktop-only. |
37-
38:Multi-project vaults use explicit selection at the agent boundary:
39:`ontology-atlas agent-brief --project SLUG` forwards the same project identity
40:as `query_ontology({ operation: "agent_brief", project: SLUG })`.
41-
42-When that brief finds incomplete `abilities` or implementation `evidence` against
43:a current source receipt, `meaningRepair:v2` gives the agent a compact human-review
44-manifest. The existing `query_ontology` tool's `meaning_repair_review` operation
45-then serves the complete typed candidates as provenance-bound pages of at most
46-20 targets and 5 KiB each, with matching full-body read calls and an opaque next
47-cursor. It stays read-only: every page and target mtime must be checked before
48-human approval, and Atlas still never patches or finalizes meaning automatically.

61-### Web and app do not promise the same screens (2026-07-27)
--
73-| Open your own markdown folder | ✅ File System Access API | ✅ absolute path | Firefox and non-FSA browsers degrade to a notice + download link |
74-| Read / edit / create nodes in that folder | ✅ | ✅ | same parser, same schema, same files |
75-| Remember the folder between visits | ❌ pick it again | ✅ | web keeps an FSA handle in its own IndexedDB; a convenience cache, not the source of truth |
76-| Work offline | ❌ | ✅ | |
77-| Git history and snapshots | ❌ degraded card + `ontology-atlas snapshot` | ✅ | a browser has no right to run git on your machine |
78:| API keys / in-app **agent** chat | ❌ **and will not be built** | ✅ native credential store | keys in browser storage leak to a single XSS, and vendors name the direct-call header `…-dangerous-direct-browser-access` |
79:| Write agent config (`.mcp.json`) into the vault | ⚠️ folder writes work, but there is no absolute path to record | ✅ | MCP registration needs a real path |
80-| In-app updates | ❌ | ✅ | |

83-surface as the desktop app. `/download` states the SmartScreen unknown-publisher
84-and managed-PC risk before the executable CTA. Chrome or Edge remains the
85-install-free fallback when that warning or an organization policy is a blocker;

113-**Meaning & time surfaces (2026-07-21 execution run, PRs #425–#449)**:
114-- **Edge popover** — edges are first-class clickable objects on the map: a
115-  click within 7px opens a popover with a plain-language sentence ("A leans
116-  on B"), the formal type, both endpoints (click = focus), the declaring
117-  `.md` (with its change-date label), an optional **why** line
118-  (`relation_notes`), and an edit-relation deep link.
119-- **Relation rationale (why)** — `relation_notes: {ref: one-line-why}` in
120:  frontmatter; MCP `add_relation` takes `why` and writes relation + note in
121-  ONE frontmatter write; `rename_concept` rewrites note keys (collision:
122-  existing new-key note wins).
123:- **Agent connect sheet** — INDEX footer agent status opens a sheet:
124:  heartbeat-file connection state, Claude Code/.mcp.json + Codex + generic
125-  registration snippets (desktop autofills the path), config-file writer on
126:  desktop, and an agent-brief preview that speaks the user's own domain
127:  names. **Scope segment** — *this folder* (the app writes `.mcp.json` /
128:  `.codex/config.toml` / `.cursor/mcp.json` / `.agents/mcp_config.json`
129-  inside the vault, so a `git diff` shows it) or *this whole computer*
130-  (the app writes nothing outside the vault; it hands you a ready command
131-  or settings block with the absolute vault path already filled in, and
132-  says plainly that a home-folder change will not appear in `git diff`).
133:  Claude Code's global path is a `claude mcp add --scope user` command
134:  because `~/.claude.json` is a file Claude Code rewrites at runtime.
135-  Default is *this folder*; your pick is remembered.
136-- **Hierarchy ink ladder** — containment edges carry depth (L0 project
137-  spine → L2 leaf) as width×value (never hue); pass-through edges (both
138-  endpoints off-screen) get demoted ink; `depends` bows are
139-  direction-consistent perpendicular offsets (mutual pairs separate).

On first visit, the folder guide sheet opens automatically first (once only, with a skip option); if the user opens a folder directly in this session, the AI agent connection sheet follows automatically once. 233-
234-- **Decision first, at full column width**: eyebrow → headline → one-paragraph lead → the macOS decision card. The card is the widest thing above the fold because it is the most important; it used to sit inside a half-width column under a taller figure.
235-- **One filled indigo button per release state, and it is the one that works.** Generated GitHub Release facts select the CTA: published assets show their real platform/architecture download and size; an unavailable asset gets honest pending copy instead of a dead release button. The releases link stays at lower weight.
236-- **Architecture help is on the page, not assumed**: "Apple menu → About This Mac; if *Chip* begins with Apple M, it's Apple Silicon". Naming both architectures and stopping there left the majority of visitors — who do not know which Mac they own — stuck in front of two buttons.
237-- **One release-state source**: everything the page may claim about a build comes from `src/views/download/model/macos-release.generated.ts`, written by `pnpm download:release-facts` out of the real GitHub Release. Published macOS → per-architecture DMGs; published Windows → the x64 NSIS installer; both carry real byte size, filename, direct URL, and copyable SHA-256. Unpublished → plain pending copy instead of placeholder facts. There is no state where the page shows a size or checksum that does not exist.
238:- **Trust is four facts with their proofs, not a paragraph**: Developer ID signing (`codesign verified`) · Apple notarization + stapling (`stapler validate passes`) · a published SHA-256 per file with the verify command built from the current version's real DMG name · and *what Atlas does not do* — no Atlas account or backend; the vault remains disk-backed and Atlas itself does not upload it. A connected coding agent may send prompts and the context or MCP results it reads to its own provider. Signing is stated as a property of the release path and drift-guarded by `release-facts.test.ts` against the real `desktop:release-artifact` chain (`desktop:sign` → `desktop:notarize` → `desktop:verify-release-dmg --require-signed --require-notarized`), so the claim cannot outlive the pipeline that backs it.
239:- **After-install path in three steps** — drag to Applications and launch · point it at a markdown folder · connect your AI assistant (tool and command counts derived from `mcp/src/index.js` and `cli/src/lib/cli-commands.mjs`, both drift-guarded) — plus the fact that makes this page a one-time visit: the installed app updates itself with one button (#726).
240:- **Windows x64 beta**: a published unsigned NSIS installer appears in its own platform section inside the same decision plate. The static warning precedes the outline CTA and names SmartScreen's unknown-publisher warning and managed-PC blocking. Native Windows CI requires dependency audits, Microsoft Defender scan, silent install, app launch, and the installed MCP sidecar smoke; it does not claim to have verified the Windows 11 SmartScreen UI.
241-- **Evidence figure**: the dogfood instrument (project hex + domain chips + hub capability circle, real `docs/ontology` census — `src/views/download/model/dogfood-census.generated.ts`, built by `scripts/build-docs-vault.mjs`) now sits beside step 02, the one place it is an answer rather than decoration, with its scope caption ("counts this repo's own vault, not yours").
242-- **Secondary CTA**: "Go to GitHub" → GitHub repo, as a visible medium outline button rather than a small source footnote.
243-- **Motion**: none on entrance (first painted frame is identical to the settled frame across every node in `#main`). The budget goes to the attention winner alone — the filled CTA eases on `--motion-base` + `--motion-ease` with a 6.1% first-frame share — and `prefers-reduced-motion` lands it instantly. The previous page inverted this: a staggered fade ran on background cards while the winner hard-cut.
244-- **Live deploy verification**: `pnpm desktop:verify-hosted` checks the deployed `wlsdks.github.io/ontology-atlas` root/download pages after the Pages workflow deploys; on a published release it also runs `pnpm desktop:verify-download` for that tag. It asserts only **server-rendered** text: a loaded-vault map hydrates client-side, so its in-app CTAs never reach the static HTML — expecting them is what kept this gate failing on every Pages deploy while the site itself was fine (5/5 runs red, 2026-07-26~27). Expected download copy is read from `messages/ko.json` instead of duplicated in the checker, so the contract is "the page renders its own copy" and cannot drift: title, source-code CTA, both platform headings, the Windows beta trust state, the hosted-site scope note, a stable GitHub Releases href, and no `/releases/latest` dependency.
245-- **Privacy note**: the installed app and vault data use local disk as the source of truth; `/docs`'s own local-source *browsing* tab stays desktop-only (unrelated to opening your primary vault from `/`)
--
262-- **Click node** → right-side panel opens (`ProjectDrawer` for project nodes, the 352px node datasheet for domain/capability/element nodes — see "Node datasheet" below)
263-- **Drag node** → reposition (releases back to physics)
264-- **Double-click node** → "local graph" mode (2-hop neighbors only, breadcrumb: `Local · Root · slugA · slugB`, click to backtrack, Esc to exit)
265-- **Right-click node** → context menu (Focus / Local graph / Copy detail URL)
266-- **Shift-click 2 nodes** → highlight shortest path
267:- **Dense-group cluster chips** → a parent with more than 12 direct children (e.g. a domain with 108 capabilities) folds its whole subtree into a single `+N` chip instead of spilling hundreds of overlapping nodes/labels. Click the chip to expand just that parent (nodes fan out as a bounded phyllotaxis disk); click the `−` chip to collapse again. Expanded parents live in the URL (`?open=slug1,slug2`) so a shared link or an AI agent reproduces the same expansion. Nested dense children get their own chips once their parent is expanded.
268-- **Expand all** → the top action opens every containment parent in one step and
269-  fits every rendered node inside the map. It is a temporary overview, not a
270-  saved default; pressing it again collapses the batch. A route arriving with
271-  existing `?open=` parents also uses full-bounds fitting on its first frame so
272-  already-open nodes do not begin off screen.
273-- **How the chip looks and where children land is a setting** (Settings › Expand, 2026-08-01 — ported from the `.qa-scratch/proto-expand.html` measurement prototype). Five values: the open control (`floating pill` · **`bar above`, default** · `shoulder badge`), the child layout (`spiral disk`, default · `fan` · `ring` · `column`), and three numbers — how many open at once (4–24, default 24), how many names are attempted per parent (3–40, default 8), and how many parents stay open at once (1–6, default 3). The default control is the bar docked directly above the **selected** node: nothing shows until you select a node, and the folded count keeps living on the node body. Rationale and the observation that would reverse it: `docs/DECISIONS.md`.
274:- **Expand realm** → focus a node (click) and an orbital **Expand realm** button appears just outside its ring (also offered as an action in the node datasheet, for container nodes). Activating it transforms the map into *that node's world*: only its containment subtree remains, re-laid-out with the node as a temporary root at the origin (children map to rings by **depth**, not kind), and everything outside unmounts behind a 1px indigo warding circle. Relations crossing the boundary fade to a stub at the ring. The transition is a 600ms choreography — outside nodes fling out along curved "gravity" trajectories, inside nodes FLIP to their new spots, the camera dollies in to fit the realm (`prefers-reduced-motion` snaps instantly). The active realm lives in the URL (`?realm=slug`) so a shared link or an AI agent reproduces the same world; a top-center **Realm: {title} ✕** chip and **Esc** (highest ladder priority) return to the full map. Click, `?open` density gating, selective ego, and top-K labels all still work inside a realm.
275-- **Ontology block exchange** — feature to exchange concept bundles folder-by-folder. INDEX's
276-  **Import Block** reads `.md` folders and, if present, `block-manifest.json`, showing **only what is coming in and what conflicts with existing files first**
277-  (dry-run — running it tentatively without writing anything). Then only the files approved by the person are written via the vault's existing `createDoc` path. **Export this realm's source .mds as a block folder** on the realm expansion screen copies only the source files of the child nodes contained in that realm. The folder picker window uses
278-  `showDirectoryPicker()` on the web, and Tauri's own picker following the same `FileSystemDirectoryHandle`
279-  protocol on the installed app. Canceling that window is neither an error nor a write. For terminal-only use, run `ontology-atlas import <path...>`.

Self-closes; owns its own Escape so the global topology Esc ladder doesn't double-fire. Desktop-only (1512/1920 scope)

311:#### Agent Panel — From What to Say First to Next Steps (2026-07-27, Desktop App Only)
312:- **Guarded ACP runtime eligibility** — The app currently offers Claude Agent for in-app ACP chat. Codex remains detected and keeps its external MCP setup path, but it is not offered in the in-app runtime selector: installed acceptance proved that Codex `read-only` can execute a self-registered Atlas MCP write without emitting the permission request the review card requires. It returns only after an app-owned MCP write gate proves reject-without-write and allow-once-with-write for both self-registered and injected servers.
313:- Clicking the **「Agent」** button in the top toolbar opens a tall vertical panel on the right side of the map. When the panel opens, the map and node info areas shift together to adjust their width. This feature is exclusive to the desktop app — browsers lack a secure place to store API keys and a valid path for requests, so the button is not rendered at all if it would do nothing.
314:- The outer dock continues to yield space to the map width, but the actual conversation surface stands as a panel with 12px spacing on the top, bottom, and right sides. Its borders, radius, and shadows share existing panel tokens used by INDEX and node details, with ACP and API-key conversations sharing the same form. The top and vertical map controls on the left side of the panel are attached 12px from each side of the seam, totaling a 24px gap, moving in sync with the dock's timing and curvature.
315-- From the first frame of opening the panel, the header, empty conversation prompt, current folder recommendations, and input box are all visible in their final positions. While waiting for connection, only the small spinner and "Connecting" status in the header move; when ready, only the text changes to "Ready." Session start occurs after the dock width movement and camera's final landing complete, ensuring map motion and process booting do not compete for the same frame.
316:- While the agent panel is open, the left INDEX temporarily collapses without changing its saved default state, yielding map width. Closing the conversation restores the original INDEX preference; opening the collapsed INDEX tab directly closes the agent panel so both auxiliary panels do not compress the map simultaneously.
317:- Thought fragments and tool calls generated from a single user message are collected into a single line of the default-collapsed **「Process · N steps」**. During execution, only the indigo dot and step count update, while the agent's response is read in a separate body. Expanding it when needed reveals both the original order and target nodes, with thought Markdown rendered as actual bold, code, and lists.
318:- GFM tables in agent responses appear as real tables with headers, row dividers, and cell padding. Long tables scroll horizontally only within the table itself, without widening the entire conversation dock.
319-- The current vault slug from `get_concept` directly selects that node on the map. The two slugs from `find_path` reveal the exact shortest path nodes and relationship lines. Only typed Atlas read tools in the same turn are used as input; response sentences or non-existent names are not used as basis for map movement. The streamed input completing the initial `tool_call` via `tool_call_update.rawInput` is also merged into the same tool row.
320-- If there is no project source connected to the starter vault, "Code Scan" is not executed first. "Connect Code Folder First" navigates to the project data sheet, and only after re-reading the connection on the same screen does the source-evidence-first build prompt appear.
321-- **First 3 words** (`buildFirstWords`) — When the conversation is empty, up to three sentences extracted from the actual state of this folder appear: ① The biggest gap in the currently viewed concept ② The concept first pointed out by the "To-do" list (the function used for judgment is the same `detectMeaningGaps`) ③ The ever-present "Where is the strangest place on this map right now?"
322:- **Zero model calls to create these sentences** — These sentences are already drawn on screen *before* the user clicks [Send]. Therefore, sending a request to create them would be an unauthorized transmission, using the user's own API costs (BYOK) without permission. The code creating these sentences is pure and does not import any transmission code (`tests/contract/agent-first-words-local.contract.test.ts`).
323-- **Clicking only fills the input box; it does not send** — Clicking places that sentence into the input box, selecting all text and focusing it. You can edit and send it or delete it. The button does not disappear even after clicking.
324-- **Does not force-fill 3 items** — In an empty folder, only one appears ("Let's organize what product we're making from scratch"); if there is no concept being viewed, ① is omitted; in a folder with nothing to fix, only ③ remains. Regardless of sentence length, **the height of one button is always the same** (measured at 1512×950: 44px for both 1 and 3 items, input box position remains unchanged).
325-- **"You can ask things like this" shown when no key or folder exists is also created by the same code** — The sentences are identical, differing only in appearance (a plain list instead of buttons). No clickable buttons are created for tasks that cannot be executed to completion.
326-- **Next step** — Makes the model mention one empty spot next to it **within the very response** where it suggests fixing the document (the `NEXT:` line in the system prompt). This line becomes a single button. By filling the input box **without calling the LLM again**, it prevents the ongoing suggestion from doubling.
327-- **Continues even if the conversation disconnects** — Starting a new conversation includes the **most recently applied changes** to this folder (git history up to 5 lines, max 120 characters per line) as context. Conversation content itself is not saved — changed content remains in frontmatter and git, becoming the context for the next conversation.
328-- **Summary of this conversation** — The subtitle in the header changes to "N concepts · M connections in this conversation" (counting only those successfully saved). Only the text changes; the line's position and size remain the same.
===== docs/ARCHITECTURE.md =====
6-# Architecture
7-
8-> 2026-07-27 update — the current architecture is a local-first, git-backed
9-> **meaning layer**: a folder of markdown files that records what each part of
10-> the product is, who owns it, what it depends on, and what proves it. People
11:> and AI coding agents read and write that same folder. Round 10 permanently
12-> removed every login and cloud-data screen. In today's route model, reading and
13:> contextual writing happen together on Topology, ACP writes pause in the same
14-> conversation for human review, and upkeep lives on the five-question Insights
15-> page. The old `/ontology/studio` and `/ontology/edit` URLs only translate old links.
16-> Earlier cloud and retired-workbench design notes are in `docs/archive/`.
17-
18-## High-level shape
--

88-
89-There is no backend, no server database, no login provider. The user's markdown
90-folder — called the **vault** everywhere in this repo — is the single source of
91:truth. Both the MCP server (used by the AI agent) and the CLI (used by the
92-developer) read and write that same folder.
93-
94-The public app/website brand is **Ontology Atlas**. The macOS app bundle,
95-bundle identifier, and DMG assets use the Ontology Atlas identity, while the
96:repo, CLI binary, and MCP package remain under `ontology-atlas`, so product
97-naming does not imply a backend, Firebase data dependency, or package rename.
98-
99-Atlas answers graph-database questions by computing the answer when asked. It
100-never keeps a second copy of the data. `compile_ontology` reads the frontmatter
101-(the `key: value` block at the top of each `.md` file) and builds one graph file
--
103-"deterministic" means here. `query_ontology` runs its graph operations over that
104-file, and write tools save confirmed changes back into the markdown.
105-
106-## Surface contract — web and app
107-
108:Decided 2026-07-27 (`docs/DECISIONS.md`); the working rule agents load is
109-`.claude/rules/surfaces.md`.
110-
111-**One codebase, one build.** The macOS app is not a port. Tauri loads the very
112-same static export into a WebView (`src-tauri/tauri.conf.json` →
113-`frontendDist: "../out"`). Nothing is forked, and nothing should be.
114-
115-**What each surface is for.** The app is where the vault lives day to day: the
116:place a person reads the map, judges it, and connects their AI agents. The web
117-has two jobs, in this order. (1) It is the **gateway** — it opens the map with
118-no install, for a demo, someone's first five minutes, or a shared link. (2) It
119-is a **second-best workbench** where no app exists yet, which in practice means
120-Chromium on Windows and Linux, because the whole web surface needs the browser's
121-File System Access API to work at all. The order matters: every visitor we have
122-observed so far arrived through the web, so treating the gateway as merely "the
123-Windows stand-in" would downgrade the only way people currently arrive. A
124-Windows app would end job 2 on that operating system; job 1 never expires.
125-
126-**The two surfaces differ in a small set of modules — we call them bridges — not
127:in the router.** Each bridge is the single file that calls a desktop-only
128-ability. The canonical roster is the bridge table in
129-`.claude/rules/surfaces.md`; the ones that shape this document are:
130-
131-| Bridge | Module | Web behaviour |
132-|---|---|---|
133-| Vault absolute path | `src/shared/lib/tauri-vault-fs.ts` | FSA handle instead (no path) |
134-| Git | `src/shared/lib/tauri-git.ts` | Cannot run → degraded card |
135-| Keychain | `src/shared/lib/tauri-secrets.ts` | Impossible by design → degraded card |
136-| LLM calls | `src/shared/lib/tauri-llm.ts` | Impossible by design → action not rendered |
137:| In-app agent runtime (ACP) | `src/shared/lib/tauri-acp.ts` | A browser cannot spawn a process → one row states why and where |
138-
139-Every bridge follows one convention: `getInvoke()` returns `null` when
140-`isTauri()` is false, and the screen then says plainly that it cannot do this
141-here. Such a screen must tell the reader two things: **why** it cannot work here
142:and **where** it can. New desktop abilities attach the same way. They do not get
143-a web version added later — that is a decision we have made, not a task waiting
144-in a backlog.
145-
146-**What "the same data" actually guarantees.** The folder on disk is the single
147-source of truth, and both surfaces open the *physically same folder* — the web
148-through an FSA handle, the app through an absolute path. The two surfaces cannot
149-end up reading the same file differently, because one contract test runs the
150:same file through all three parsers (web, MCP, scripts) and the schema is
151:written in exactly one place, `mcp/src/schema.mjs`. Anything that has to pass
152-between the two surfaces is written **inside the vault** as plain text —
153-`.ontology-atlas/activity.jsonl` and `llm-audit.jsonl`. That is the rule this
154-contract sets: *anything shared between surfaces lives in the vault folder.* If
155:two people (or an agent and a person) edit at once,
156-`patch_concept(expected_mtime)` stops the second write from silently erasing the
157-first.
158-
159-Three things are deliberately **not** shared. The "last opened vault" handle:
160-each surface keeps its own IndexedDB, so you pick the folder once per surface,
--
166-**Verification is split three ways** (this replaced the old web↔app round-trip
167-check, which the same decision abolished). First, screens that both surfaces
168-share are tested once in the browser, and that counts as proof for the app too,
169-because it is literally the same bundle — the exceptions are font rendering,
170-scrolling, and the window frame, which still have to be checked in the installed
171:app. Second, desktop-only abilities are proved *only* in the installed app.
172-Third, the web surface has its own test, `tests/e2e/web-surface-smoke.spec.ts`,
173-wired into `.github/workflows/e2e.yml` on a deliberately wider condition than
174-the rest of the suite — nobody watches the web by hand, so that test is its only
175-guard.
176-
177-A deep link built on one surface is not guaranteed to rebuild the same screen on
178-the other. Where such links work, treat it as a convenience, not a promise.
179-
180:### In-app coding agents over ACP (2026-08-16)
181-
182:The app can launch a coding agent the user already installed (Claude Code,
183:Codex, …) and speak the Agent Client Protocol v1 to it over stdio. It is a
184:desktop-only ability, so it attaches through the bridge convention above. Both
185-decision records behind it are in `docs/DECISIONS.md`, dated 2026-08-16.
186-
187-| Module | Owns |
188-|---|---|
189:| `src-tauri/src/acp.rs` | Registry snapshot parsing, runtime detection (`ready` · `cli-missing` · `node-missing` · `uvx-missing` · `binary-missing`), launch resolution, isolated config + credential symlink, permission verdict, process-group termination |
190:| `src-tauri/src/lib.rs` | The five Tauri commands (`acp_detect_runtimes`, `acp_start`, `acp_send`, `acp_stop`, `acp_permission_verdict`), the four `acp://*` events, and the vault-root rejection that also guards a session's working directory |
191:| `src-tauri/src/acp-registry.json` | The committed registry snapshot, generated by `scripts/build-acp-registry.mjs` (`pnpm acp:registry`); icons are fetched at build time into `public/acp-icons/` |
192:| `src/shared/lib/tauri-acp.ts` | The capability bridge and its web degradation. It never re-implements the permission policy |
193:| `src/features/acp-session/` | `acp-client.ts` (JSON-RPC framing over the bridge) and `use-acp-session.ts` (one session's lifetime; a permission request blocks until the screen answers, and an unanswered one is refused) |
194:| `src/widgets/acp-chat-panel/` | `AcpChatPanel.tsx` and `AcpPermissionCard.tsx`. **No route or control opens this panel yet**, so it is a module, not a surface |
195:| `src/widgets/app-settings-menu/ui/AcpRuntimeSettings.tsx` | The one ACP surface a user can reach today: the settings sheet's Runtime section |
196-
197-Three properties are structural rather than incidental. The registry is a
198:committed snapshot, so no ACP code path touches the network at runtime. A
199-launched session runs against an app-owned config directory rather than the
200-user's global one, which is what makes the permission gate exist at all;
201-credentials are symlinked, never copied. And the child runs in its own process
202-group, so quitting the app ends the adapter and everything it spawned.
203-
--
229-   resolves aliases, collects the problems it found, lists the frontmatter
230-   arrays it would tidy up, computes a stable `graphHash` (one fingerprint for
231-   the meaning of the whole graph), and optionally builds query indexes.
232-4. `query_ontology` serves graph operations such as `neighbors`, `path`,
233-   `project_scope`, `blast_radius`, `cycles`, `maintenance_plan`,
234:   `workspace_brief`, and `health`. `agent_brief` accepts an explicit project
235-   in multi-project vaults and derives a fresh categorical `meaningAssessment`
236-   for that project; it does not reuse a saved score.
237-5. Write tools change the markdown only when someone explicitly calls
238-   add/patch/relation/rename/merge/delete. Analysis tools such as
239-   `analyze_repo_structure` and `infer_imports` only propose candidates; they
--
254-Every node carries two names. The `uid` is a lowercase UUIDv4 that never
255-changes — it is the node's permanent identity. The `slug` is the short readable
256-name in the file path and the URL, and it can be renamed at any time. The
257-compiler requires UID validity and uniqueness,
258-includes UID and merge-owned `merged_uids` in the semantic hash, and exposes
259:`uidToSlug`, `slugToUid`, and `mergedUidToSlug`. Compiled nodes and agent-facing
260-node summaries carry both values. Relations, adjacency maps, edge endpoints,
261:URLs, and canvas IDs remain slug-based; exact MCP identity lookup and interop
262-exports use UID. JSON-LD/GraphML serialize identity as `urn:uuid:<uid>`.
263-Rename/reclassify preserve UID; merge preserves the survivor and absorbs source
264-identity history. Invalid identity aborts compilation rather than producing a
265-partial artifact.
266-
--
269-1. `useLocalVault()` returns `{ status: 'loaded', handle, manifest }`.
270-2. `useDataSourceMode()` returns `'local'`.
271-3. `useProjects()` derives projects from `manifest.docs` (filter `kind: project`).
272-4. `useOntologyInsight()` derives ontology nodes/edges from vault frontmatter.
273-5. Mutations (`useProjectMutations.updateProject`) write directly to `.md`
274:   files — the installed desktop app uses the Tauri native vault bridge,
275-   hosted web uses the browser File System Access API directly (root-first-open,
276-   2026-07: `FirstRunStarterModule` in the topology hub's INDEX panel calls
277-   `useLocalVault().open()` on `/` itself, no install required). `/docs`'s own
278-   separate local-source tab (browsing a second vault as a documentation
279:   source inside that page) stays desktop-gated — that is a narrower, older
280-   feature unrelated to opening your primary vault from the map.
281-6. Ontology block import/export follows the same split: browser
282-   `showDirectoryPicker()` or the Tauri `FileSystemDirectoryHandle` shim feeds
283-   one recursive block reader/writer. Import plans conflicts before writing;
284-   native or browser picker cancellation has no side effect.
285-
286:The MCP server is independent: it reads the same vault directory through the
287:filesystem (Node.js `fs`), not the WebView bridge. AI agents and the installed app end up with the same view.

===== docs/guide/trust.md =====
2-
3-It involves pulling binaries from anonymous repositories onto your own computer. Therefore, we start by listing what it does not do.
4-
5-## Local source of truth, explicit provider boundary
6-
7:The vault's canonical files remain on your disk. Atlas has no backend, account, or telemetry, and opening or editing a folder does not upload it to Atlas.
8-
9-## It is plain Markdown
10-
11-Even if you delete the app, the folder remains. Nothing gets locked. This is not marketing copy; it is a structural fact. Because the storage format is Markdown, there is no way to lock it.
12-
--
14-
15-It reads only the folders you choose. Dotfiles like `.env` and `.git/` are excluded from indexing. It does not look at anything else on your disk.
16-
17-## AI connectivity is optional
18-
19:Atlas-controlled API and runner calls state and record their destination in `.ontology-atlas/llm-audit.jsonl`. ACP and externally connected MCP agents are a separate boundary: Atlas MCP stays local over stdio, but the selected coding agent may send prompts, context, and tool results to its provider. Atlas does not claim that provider-owned traffic is recorded in its LLM audit log.
20-
21-## Signing and Notarization
22-
23-The macOS app is signed with Apple Developer ID and notarized. It opens without Gatekeeper warnings.
24-

4-identity for the `ontology-atlas` project. `ontology-atlas` stays the
5:repository, CLI, and MCP package name so existing terminal and agent package
6-contracts remain stable.
7-The current release asset identity is `ontology-atlas`.

9-`ontology-atlas` can become a macOS-installed app without changing the source of
10-truth. The desktop app should be a native shell around the same local markdown
--
25-- The desktop picker persists a small recent-vault list and can reopen a recent
26-  Tauri vault from its stored local path, so closing one vault does not force the
27-  user back through Finder every time.
28-- Recent desktop vaults can also be removed from the picker, so stale paths from
29-  moved or deleted folders do not trap the user in a broken first-run loop.
30:- Stored Tauri vault paths are ignored outside the Tauri runtime, so the hosted
31-  website cannot revive installed-app recents as writable browser vault state.
32:- The local vault, CLI graph engine, and MCP setup gates remain the authority.
33-- Electron stays a fallback if a later slice needs bundled Node.js behavior.
34-
35-## Product Quality Bar
36-
37-The target is not a thin website wrapper. The macOS app should feel credible
38-next to Obsidian, Claude Desktop, and Codex Desktop:
39-
40-- native installation with a stable `.app` launch path, dock behavior, window
41:  sizing, recent vault recall, and clear local permission prompts.
42:- first-run setup that explains the vault folder, CLI, and MCP handoff without
43-  sending the user back to hosted docs for the core path.
44-- local-file confidence: the user can see which vault is open, where data is
45-  stored, and what will be written before ontology edits touch markdown.
46:- agent confidence: Claude Code and Codex setup checks remain one click or one
47:  copied command away, and desktop smoke must include MCP verification.
48-- offline usefulness: `/docs`, `/topology`, `/ontology/studio`, and
49-  `/ontology/insights` remain usable from the packaged app against the local
50-  vault; legacy `/ontology` and `/ontology/edit` links still converge on
51-  Topology and Workshop instead of breaking.

89-- the root package stays free of Firebase SDK, Firebase Admin, and Firebase CLI
90-  dependencies (SDK ban). The hosted website deploys via the separate GitHub
91-  Pages workflow, not the local-only app package.
92:- `docs-vault:check`, `cli:mcp-verify`, `desktop:doctor`, `desktop:dev`,
93-  `desktop:smoke`, `desktop:verify-app`, `desktop:build:app`,
94-  `desktop:build`, `desktop:release-source`, `desktop:release-tag`,
95-  `desktop:release-github`, `desktop:release-run`, `desktop:release-status`, `desktop:sign`,
96-  `desktop:notarize`, `desktop:verify-dmg`, `desktop:verify-install` are

101-- `src-tauri/tauri.conf.json` loads `../out`, runs `pnpm build` before
102-  packaging, and targets the macOS `.app` bundle.
103-- the app bundle metadata identifies the install as a `DeveloperTool` with a
104-  local-first ontology workbench description, so Finder/Gatekeeper-facing
105-  metadata does not ship as an anonymous web wrapper.
--
109-- the Tauri WebView CSP is enabled instead of left open: it allows local app
110-  assets, data/blob images, local styles, and the Tauri IPC endpoint required by
111-  native vault commands, without allowing arbitrary remote hosts.
112-- `src-tauri/capabilities/default.json` stays scoped to the `main` window, but
113-  no longer through the `core:default` umbrella: that umbrella expands to nine
114:  permission sets, and four of them — `core:image`, `core:resources`,
115-  `core:menu`, `core:tray` — were granted to a webview that never called them.
116-  The capability now enumerates five core sets (`core:path:default`,
117-  `core:event:default`, `core:window:default`, `core:webview:default`,
118:  `core:app:default`) plus `updater:default` and `process:allow-restart` for the
119-  one-button update-and-restart path. Only two of those five have an observed
120-  frontend caller — `listen` needs `core:event` and `getVersion` needs
121-  `core:app`. The other three are retained deliberately: dropping them is a
122:  further narrowing, and narrowing a permission without installed-app proof
123-  fails silently, which is the one failure mode a capability gate cannot catch. The
124-  desktop app still does not grant broad Tauri filesystem, shell, HTTP, or
125:  opener plugin permissions; `desktop:check` allows permissions only by name
126-  and blocks those families by prefix.
127-- `src-tauri/src/lib.rs` exposes the local vault bridge used by the installed
128-  app: native folder selection, recursive directory listing, markdown/image
129-  reads, text writes, file and directory deletion, directory creation, and
130-  existence checks.
131-- `src/shared/lib/tauri-vault-fs.ts` wraps those commands as a
132-  `FileSystemDirectoryHandle`-compatible shim through the supported
133-  `@tauri-apps/api/core` `invoke` / `isTauri` API, so the desktop app reuses
134-  the same `buildLocalManifest`, editor save, image preview, conflict guard, and
135:  agent-config bootstrap flows that the web prototype uses without depending on
136-  private Tauri WebView internals. The same shim now exposes the `values()`
137-  iterator used by ontology-block recursion, so INDEX block import and realm
138-  block export use a purpose-titled native folder picker in the installed app
139-  instead of being disabled when WebView `showDirectoryPicker()` is absent.
140-  Import still opens a dry-run merge preview before any vault write; cancelling
--
148-  rendering the hosted marketing page inside the installed app.
149-- `src/views/docs-vault/ui/DocsVaultPage.tsx` treats that desktop intent as the
150-  installed-app first-run path and shows a vault setup welcome before opening
151-  the native picker from an explicit user action.
152-- `pnpm test:desktop:bridge` verifies that shim against the Tauri command names,
153:  vault-local agent config validation, and Rust unit tests for the relative-path guard that keeps file
154-  operations inside the selected vault root. The Rust guard canonicalizes
155-  existing targets and nearest existing parents so symlinks inside the vault
156-  cannot redirect read, write, mkdir, exists, or remove operations outside the
157-  selected root. Write-target parent directories are checked before
158-  `create_dir_all`, so a symlinked vault directory cannot create folders outside

163-  checksum file next to the DMG.

165-  long enough to catch early Tauri/WebView startup crashes, then terminates it.
166-  Use `--kill-existing --open-app --require-window
167-  --require-capturable-window --require-accessibility-window` when checking the actual LaunchServices app
168:  window after iterative local builds so stale processes from the same bundle do

170-  window appears or no screenshot artifact can be produced for that window.
171-- `scripts/verify-macos-dmg.mjs` verifies that the `.sha256` line names the DMG
172-  basename, checks the bytes, runs `hdiutil verify`, mounts the image read-only,
173-  and checks for `Ontology Atlas.app` plus the Applications symlink pointing to
--
202-- `.github/workflows/release-macos.yml` removes the temporary Developer ID
203-  keychain and decoded `.p12` with an `always()` cleanup step after the per-arch
204-  artifact handoff, so failed signing/notarization attempts do not leave release
205-  credentials on the runner filesystem for the rest of the job.
206-- `scripts/sign-macos-app.mjs` deeply signs the built `.app` with hardened
207:  runtime using `APPLE_SIGNING_IDENTITY`, then runs strict deep `codesign`
208-  verification.
209-- `scripts/notarize-macos-dmg.mjs` submits the DMG with `xcrun notarytool`,
210-  waits for notarization, staples and validates the ticket, then refreshes the
211-  `.sha256` file because stapling changes the DMG bytes. Notary failure logs
212-  redact Apple ID, app-specific password, and keychain profile arguments before
--
253-  sessions keep `/docs` in the read-only packaged docs mode, disable the local
254-  vault source, and point users back to the macOS download path instead of
255-  calling the browser folder picker.
256-- `pnpm desktop:release-preflight` is the local operator shortcut before

264-  Developer ID credentials, so it is the fast local proof for an unsigned
