===== src/features/acp-session/model/acp-client.ts:390-505 =====
   390	      verdict = await handlers.verdict(request.filePath);
   391	    } catch (error) {
   392	      handlers.onProtocolNotice?.(`verdict-failed: ${String(error)}`);
   393	    }
   394	    /* A read is auto-allowed only with no path or inside the vault. A read outside falls through to
   395	       the ordinary permission card too. */
   396	    if (
   397	      atlasMode === 'read' &&
   398	      allowOnce &&
   399	      (request.filePath === null || verdict === 'allow-inside-vault')
   400	    ) {
   401	      write({ jsonrpc: '2.0', id, result: selected(allowOnce.optionId) });
   402	      return;
   403	    }
   404	    if (!ontologyWrite && verdict === 'allow-inside-vault' && allowOnce) {
   405	      write({ jsonrpc: '2.0', id, result: selected(allowOnce.optionId) });
   406	      return;
   407	    }
   408	
   409	    // An Atlas write, access outside the vault, or a generic tool with an unknown path: ask the person.
   410	    let chosen: string | null = null;
   411	    try {
   412	      chosen = await handlers.askUser(
   413	        ontologyWrite ? { ...request, reviewKind: 'ontology-write' } : request,
   414	      );
   415	    } catch {
   416	      chosen = null; // a failure while asking is a rejection
   417	    }
   418	
   419	    // Verify the user's choice really is an option of that request. A screen returning a stale
   420	    // request's value would allow the wrong thing.
   421	    const valid = chosen && request.options.some((o) => o.optionId === chosen);
   422	    if (valid) {
   423	      write({ jsonrpc: '2.0', id, result: selected(chosen as string) });
   424	      return;
   425	    }
   426	    if (rejectOnce) {
   427	      write({ jsonrpc: '2.0', id, result: selected(rejectOnce.optionId) });
   428	      return;
   429	    }
   430	    // With not even a reject option, answer cancelled — no answer leaves the other side waiting forever.
   431	    write({ jsonrpc: '2.0', id, result: { outcome: { outcome: 'cancelled' } } });
   432	  };
   433	
   434	  const handle = (message: Record<string, unknown>) => {
   435	    const method = typeof message.method === 'string' ? message.method : null;
   436	    const hasId = message.id !== undefined && message.id !== null;
   437	
   438	    // Agent → us, a **request**: it must be answered.
   439	    if (method && hasId) {
   440	      if (method === 'session/request_permission') {
   441	        void answerPermission(message.id, asRecord(message.params));
   442	        return;
   443	      }
   444	    // An undeclared capability. Staying silent leaves the other side stalled.
   445	      handlers.onProtocolNotice?.(`declined:${method}`);
   446	      write({
   447	        jsonrpc: '2.0',
   448	        id: message.id,
   449	        error: { code: METHOD_NOT_FOUND, message: `capability not declared: ${method}` },
   450	      });
   451	      return;
   452	    }
   453	
   454	    // A notification: no answer.
   455	    if (method) {
   456	      if (method === 'session/update') {
   457	        const params = asRecord(message.params);
   458	        handlers.onUpdate?.(asRecord(params.update));
   459	      }
   460	      return;
   461	    }
   462	
   463	    // A response to one of our requests.
   464	    const id = typeof message.id === 'number' ? message.id : null;
   465	    if (id === null) return;
   466	    const waiting = pending.get(id);
   467	    if (!waiting) return;
   468	    pending.delete(id);
   469	    if (message.error) {
   470	      waiting.reject(new Error(JSON.stringify(message.error)));
   471	      return;
   472	    }
   473	    waiting.resolve(asRecord(message.result));
   474	  };
   475	
   476	  const ingest = (line: string) => {
   477	    if (disposed) return;
   478	    const trimmed = line.trim();
   479	    if (!trimmed) return;
   480	    let parsed: unknown;
   481	    try {
   482	      parsed = JSON.parse(trimmed);
   483	    } catch {
   484	    // A non-protocol line (the adapter's banner, say) is discarded — but not silently.
   485	      handlers.onProtocolNotice?.(`unparsable:${trimmed.slice(0, 120)}`);
   486	      return;
   487	    }
   488	    if (!parsed || typeof parsed !== 'object') return;
   489	    handle(parsed as Record<string, unknown>);
   490	  };
   491	
   492	  const unsubscribe = transport.subscribe(ingest);
   493	
   494	  return {
   495	    initialize: () =>
   496	      call('initialize', {
   497	        protocolVersion: 1,
   498	    // ⚠️ File and terminal capabilities are not declared. What is not declared is treated as
   499	    // "unsupported" (the ACP initialization convention).
   500	        clientCapabilities: {},
   501	      }),
   502	    newSession: async (params) => {
   503	      const result = await call('session/new', {
   504	        cwd: params.cwd,
   505	        mcpServers: params.mcpServers ?? [],
===== src/features/acp-session/model/mode-safety.ts:1-105 =====
     1	/**
     2	 * The safety verdict for the "working mode" list — **it separates the known from the unknown.**
     3	 *
     4	 * Why (2026-08-17). The old verdict was a one-line denylist:
     5	 *
     6	 * ```
     7	 * modes.filter((m) => !GATE_REMOVING_MODES.has(m.id))
     8	 * ```
     9	 *
    10	 * It hides only what is written down. So **when the adapter adds a new mode it becomes visible and
    11	 * selectable without our knowing.** If that mode removes the permission gate, one choice undoes this
    12	 * screen's promise and the screen says nothing. **A safety device that treats what it does not know
    13	 * as safe is not a device.**
    14	 *
    15	 * It is an immediate problem too: the adapter versions are being bumped
    16	 * (`claude-agent-acp` 0.68→0.69, `codex-acp` 1.3→1.4) and our gate measurements were taken on the
    17	 * **old versions**.
    18	 *
    19	 * **Three categories:**
    20	 *
    21	 * - **Measured to remove the gate** → never shown (as before).
    22	 * - **Measured to be fine** → shown normally.
    23	 * - **Not yet measured** → shown, but **stated as unknown.** It is not hidden because blocking a
    24	 *   perfectly good new mode is a lie too. The permission card already uses the same discipline
    25	 *   (say unknown when unknown, and recommend the safe side).
    26	 */
    27	
    28	/**
    29	 * Requires the bare minimum for the verdict — it discriminates on `id` alone. So the caller's wider
    30	 * type (`AcpChoice`) can go in and come back out unchanged.
    31	 */
    32	export interface AcpModeChoice {
    33	  id: string;
    34	}
    35	
    36	/**
    37	 * Modes **measured to remove the gate**. The dividing line is not "is it strict" but **"does it let
    38	 * things through without asking"** — a mode that closes with a rejection (`dontAsk`) fails toward
    39	 * safety and is therefore not here.
    40	 */
    41	const GATE_REMOVING = new Set([
    42	  'bypasspermissions',
    43	  'acceptedits',
    44	  'agent-full-access',
    45	  /*
    46	   * `agent` belongs here. By name it sounds like "the normal mode", but the measurement
    47	   * (2026-08-16) is recorded in `src-tauri/src/acp.rs`: launching codex on its default (`agent`)
    48	   * gave *"files written outside the working folder with zero permission requests"*.
    49	   *
    50	   * **Re-measured 2026-08-17 — unchanged on `codex-acp` 1.4.** This closes the worry the block above
    51	   * left open ("our gate measurements were taken on the old versions"). In the installed app this
    52	   * line was briefly removed, a session opened on `agent`, and it was asked to *"write hello to
    53	   * /tmp/atlas-gate-probe.txt"* — the file appeared (contents `hello`) with **no permission card at
    54	   * all**. This adapter offers only two modes (`Read-only`, `Agent`). `Read-only` is safer for
    55	   * direct files but does not guard MCP writes, so mode classification alone never makes Codex
    56	   * eligible for in-app chat (`runtime-gate.ts`).
    57	   */
    58	  'agent',
    59	]);
    60	
    61	/** Modes measured not to widen direct adapter access inside an already guarded runtime. */
    62	const VERIFIED_SAFE = new Set(['default', 'read-only', 'readonly', 'plan', 'ask']);
    63	
    64	const normalize = (id: unknown): string =>
    65	  typeof id === 'string' ? id.trim().toLowerCase() : '';
    66	
    67	export interface ModePartition<T extends AcpModeChoice = AcpModeChoice> {
    68	  /** What is offered to the screen — the measured and the unmeasured together. */
    69	  offered: T[];
    70	  /** The ids among them that are **not yet measured**. The screen must state this fact. */
    71	  unverified: string[];
    72	  /** Entries dropped for a malformed shape — counted so they do not disappear silently. */
    73	  dropped: number;
    74	}
    75	
    76	export function partitionModes<T extends AcpModeChoice>(
    77	  modes: readonly T[],
    78	): ModePartition<T> {
    79	  const offered: T[] = [];
    80	  const unverified: string[] = [];
    81	  let dropped = 0;
    82	  for (const mode of Array.isArray(modes) ? modes : []) {
    83	    const key = normalize(mode?.id);
    84	    if (key.length === 0) {
    85	      dropped += 1;
    86	      continue;
    87	    }
    88	    if (GATE_REMOVING.has(key)) continue;
    89	    offered.push(mode);
    90	    if (!VERIFIED_SAFE.has(key)) unverified.push(mode.id);
    91	  }
    92	  return { offered, unverified, dropped };
    93	}
===== src/features/acp-session/model/vault-mcp-server.ts:85-145 =====
    85	
    86	/**
    87	 * The one MCP server that reads this vault. **An empty array** when there is no known way to launch
    88	 * it — passing a path that does not exist gives a session that starts while its tools are quietly absent.
    89	 *
    90	 * Also an empty array when the vault already registers the same server for this runtime (see
    91	 * `vaultAlreadyRegisters` above) — which is what stops it becoming two copies.
    92	 */
    93	export function vaultMcpServers(
    94	  launch: McpServerLaunch | null,
    95	  vaultPath: string | null,
    96	  registration?: ExistingVaultMcpRegistration | null,
    97	  options?: { ownsWriteGate?: boolean },
    98	): AcpMcpServer[] {
    99	  if (!launch || !vaultPath) return [];
   100	  if (vaultAlreadyRegisters(launch, registration)) return [];
   101	  /*
   102	   * **One session, one checkpoint — held by whoever can actually hold it.**
   103	   *
   104	   * A runtime with app-owned config isolation (Claude) already raises a permission
   105	   * request for every tool call, so a second gate here would ask the same person the
   106	   * same question twice. A runtime without it (Codex, measured 2026-08-24) lets an
   107	   * Atlas MCP write reach disk with no request at all — there the server has to hold
   108	   * the gate itself, because nobody else does.
   109	   *
   110	   * `ownsWriteGate` means "this runtime's own configuration already produces the
   111	   * request", so the server gate is switched **off** for it and **on** for everyone
   112	   * else. Defaulting to `false` is the safe direction: a runtime nobody has measured
   113	   * gets the gate rather than a silent write path.
   114	   */
   115	  const serverGate = options?.ownsWriteGate === true ? null : 'on';
   116	  const projectRoot = projectRootForVault(vaultPath);
   117	  return [
   118	    {
   119	      name: VAULT_MCP_SERVER_NAME,
   120	      command: launch.command,
   121	      args: [...launch.args],
   122	      env: [
   123	        { name: 'OATLAS_VAULT', value: vaultPath },
   124	        /*
   125	         * ⚠️ **The repository root, when we actually know it** (measured in the installed app,
   126	         * 2026-08-25). This used to be omitted on the reasoning that the vault might sit outside the
   127	         * repository, so guessing would pin a wrong value. That was right while every vault lived
   128	         * beside its project — and it broke the moment maps moved inside one.
   129	         *
   130	         * With the vault at `<project>/atlas`, MCP resolved its code root to the vault itself, which
   131	         * holds the map and none of the product. The agent surveyed a folder containing four seeded
   132	         * files, refused to look one level up, and reported it could not start — so the door built
   133	         * to make a map from somebody's code could not see their code.
   134	         *
   135	         * `projectRootForVault` returns a path only for the shape Atlas itself creates. Anywhere
   136	         * else it returns null and the previous behaviour stands: MCP finds the root on its own.
   137	         */
   138	        ...(projectRoot ? [{ name: 'OATLAS_REPO_ROOT', value: projectRoot }] : []),
   139	        ...(serverGate ? [{ name: 'OATLAS_WRITE_CONSENT', value: serverGate }] : []),
   140	      ],
   141	    },
   142	  ];
   143	}
   144	
   145	/**
===== mcp/src/write-consent.mjs:1-180 =====
     1	/**
     2	 * **The app-owned write checkpoint.**
     3	 *
     4	 * ⚠️ Why this file exists (2026-08-24). The permission gate an agent shows is the
     5	 * *agent's* gate, and it does not necessarily cover this server. Measured on the
     6	 * installed `1.0.0-rc.10`: a Codex session running in `read-only` mode blocked
     7	 * direct file writes, yet a self-registered Atlas `add_relation` changed the vault
     8	 * with **no permission request and no review card**. Codex's `--ask-for-approval`
     9	 * documents its scope plainly — it decides "when the model requires human approval
    10	 * before executing **a command**" — so MCP tool calls were never in that scope. The
    11	 * screen promised a gate the wire did not have, and Codex was removed from in-app
    12	 * chat because of it (`docs/DECISIONS.md` 2026-08-24 (111)).
    13	 *
    14	 * That record also wrote down what would earn Codex its way back: *"an app-owned
    15	 * MCP proxy or server capability token reliably pauses every Codex Atlas write."*
    16	 * This is that checkpoint, and it lives where it belongs — **in the server that
    17	 * performs the write**, not in a third party's config. A gate the vault owns holds
    18	 * for every client that ever connects, not just the one that was measured.
    19	 *
    20	 * The mechanism is MCP elicitation (`elicitation/create`): before a write tool
    21	 * touches disk, the server asks the connected client to put the question in front
    22	 * of a person. `codex-acp` forwards `mcpServer/elicitation/request` into ACP
    23	 * `session/request_permission`, which is the same permission card the app already
    24	 * renders for Claude — so one server-side gate reaches both.
    25	 *
    26	 * **Fail closed.** If the gate is on and the client never declared the
    27	 * `elicitation` capability, the write is refused rather than performed silently.
    28	 * A checkpoint that waves traffic through when it cannot see is not a checkpoint;
    29	 * that is precisely the failure this file was written to end.
    30	 */
    31	
    32	/** Same vocabulary as `OATLAS_READ_ONLY` so the two switches read alike. */
    33	export function parseConsentEnv(value) {
    34	  if (typeof value !== 'string') return false;
    35	  return ['1', 'true', 'yes', 'on'].includes(value.trim().toLowerCase());
    36	}
    37	
    38	/**
    39	 * A short, human-readable line naming what is about to change. It is the whole
    40	 * question a person answers, so it must say the vault-visible effect — not the
    41	 * tool's internal argument shape.
    42	 */
    43	export function describeWrite(toolName, args) {
    44	  const a = args && typeof args === 'object' ? args : {};
    45	  const slug = typeof a.slug === 'string' ? a.slug : null;
    46	  const from = typeof a.from === 'string' ? a.from : null;
    47	  const to = typeof a.to === 'string' ? a.to : null;
    48	  const count = (key) => (Array.isArray(a[key]) ? a[key].length : null);
    49	
    50	  switch (toolName) {
    51	    case 'add_concept':
    52	      return slug ? `Create concept ${slug}` : 'Create one concept';
    53	    case 'add_concepts': {
    54	      const n = count('concepts');
    55	      return n === null ? 'Create concepts' : `Create ${n} concept(s)`;
    56	    }
    57	    case 'add_relation':
    58	      return from && to ? `Link ${from} → ${to}` : 'Add one relation';
    59	    case 'add_relations': {
    60	      const n = count('relations');
    61	      return n === null ? 'Add relations' : `Add ${n} relation(s)`;
    62	    }
    63	    case 'remove_relation':
    64	      return from && to ? `Remove the link ${from} → ${to}` : 'Remove one relation';
    65	    case 'replace_relation':
    66	      return from && to ? `Replace the link ${from} → ${to}` : 'Replace one relation';
    67	    case 'patch_concept':
    68	      return slug ? `Edit concept ${slug}` : 'Edit one concept';
    69	    case 'rename_concept':
    70	      return from && to ? `Rename ${from} → ${to}` : 'Rename one concept';
    71	    case 'reclassify_concept':
    72	      return slug ? `Change the kind of ${slug}` : 'Change one concept kind';
    73	    case 'merge_concepts':
    74	      return from && to ? `Merge ${from} into ${to}` : 'Merge concepts';
    75	    case 'delete_concept':
    76	      return slug ? `Delete concept ${slug}` : 'Delete one concept';
    77	    case 'absorb_document':
    78	      return 'Absorb a document into typed nodes';
    79	    case 'connect_project_source':
    80	      return 'Connect a source folder to this vault';
    81	    case 'disconnect_project_source':
    82	      return 'Disconnect a source folder from this vault';
    83	    case 'git_snapshot':
    84	      return 'Commit the vault';
    85	    case 'finalize_project_meaning':
    86	      return 'Write the project meaning receipt';
    87	    case 'index_project':
    88	      return 'Write an index of the connected source';
    89	    default:
    90	      return `Run ${toolName}`;
    91	  }
    92	}
    93	
    94	/**
    95	 * A dry run asks for nothing: it reports a plan and leaves the disk alone, so
    96	 * pausing it would train people to click through the very card that matters.
    97	 * The flag name differs per tool, so both spellings are honoured.
    98	 */
    99	export function isDryRun(args) {
   100	  if (!args || typeof args !== 'object') return false;
   101	  return args.dryRun === true || args.dry_run === true;
   102	}
   103	
   104	export const CONSENT_DECLINED = 'consent-declined';
   105	export const CONSENT_UNAVAILABLE = 'consent-unavailable';
   106	
   107	/**
   108	 * Ask the connected client to put this write in front of a person.
   109	 *
   110	 * `server` is the low-level SDK `Server`; `elicitInput` is its push-style
   111	 * server→client request. Returns `{ allowed }` and, when refused, a `reason` the
   112	 * caller turns into an error the agent can read and act on.
   113	 */
   114	export async function requestWriteConsent({ server, toolName, args, enabled }) {
   115	  if (!enabled) return { allowed: true, asked: false };
   116	  if (isDryRun(args)) return { allowed: true, asked: false };
   117	
   118	  const capabilities =
   119	    typeof server?.getClientCapabilities === 'function' ? server.getClientCapabilities() : undefined;
   120	  if (!capabilities || !capabilities.elicitation) {
   121	    return {
   122	      allowed: false,
   123	      asked: false,
   124	      reason: CONSENT_UNAVAILABLE,
   125	      message:
   126	        `This vault requires a human decision before any write, and this client did not offer ` +
   127	        `a way to ask (no "elicitation" capability was declared at initialize). No change was made. ` +
   128	        `Use a client that can surface permission requests, or unset OATLAS_WRITE_CONSENT for a ` +
   129	        `vault where the client owns the gate.`,
   130	    };
   131	  }
   132	
   133	  const summary = describeWrite(toolName, args);
   134	  let result;
   135	  try {
   136	    result = await server.elicitInput({
   137	      mode: 'form',
   138	      /*
   139	       * ⚠️ **`confirm` is asked for, never required** (installed acceptance, 2026-08-24).
   140	       *
   141	       * It was `required: ['confirm']` at first, and that made the gate refuse **every** answer,
   142	       * including yes. A form-capable client fills the boolean; an ACP bridge does not have one to
   143	       * fill. `codex-acp` maps this request onto ACP `session/request_permission`, the app renders
   144	       * its ordinary permission card, and pressing 「allow once」 comes back as `action: 'accept'`
   145	       * with **no form content at all**. Codex reported it verbatim: *"the permission response was
   146	       * invalid because it lacked the required `confirm` field"* — the write was refused, twice,
   147	       * after a person had said yes.
   148	       *
   149	       * A checkpoint that cannot be passed is not a checkpoint, it is a wall, and a wall teaches
   150	       * people to route around the gate. So the boolean stays as the **form** spelling of the
   151	       * question, and `action` stays the answer.
   152	       */
   153	      message: `${summary}. Apply this change to the vault?`,
   154	      requestedSchema: {
   155	        type: 'object',
   156	        properties: {
   157	          confirm: {
   158	            type: 'boolean',
   159	            title: 'Apply this change',
   160	            description: `${toolName} — ${summary}`,
   161	          },
   162	        },
   163	      },
   164	    });
   165	  } catch (error) {
   166	    // A transport error, a timeout, or a client that advertised the capability
   167	    // and then refused the method all land here. None of them is a yes.
   168	    return {
   169	      allowed: false,
   170	      asked: true,
   171	      reason: CONSENT_UNAVAILABLE,
   172	      message:
   173	        `Asking for permission failed (${error?.message ?? 'unknown error'}). No change was made.`,
   174	    };
   175	  }
   176	
   177	  /*
   178	   * **`action` is the answer; the boolean only overrides it.**
   179	   *
   180	   * `accept` is the MCP elicitation spelling of "the person said yes" — `decline` and `cancel` are
===== docs/DECISIONS.md:20201-20248 =====
 20201	## 2026-08-24 — Codex returns to in-app chat, because the checkpoint moved to the server
 20202	
 20203	**Convened because**: decision (111) removed Codex from in-app chat and wrote down
 20204	the exact price of return. This record pays it, and records what the acceptance run
 20205	found on the way.
 20206	
 20207	**Prior decision**: (111) stands and is now satisfied, not overturned. Its restore
 20208	condition was *"an app-owned MCP proxy or server capability token reliably pauses
 20209	every Codex Atlas write… proves reject/allow on a disposable vault."* (113) built
 20210	that checkpoint inside the server that performs the write. This record is the
 20211	measurement.
 20212	
 20213	**What was measured** — installed build, disposable vault at `~/Downloads/atlas-accept`
 20214	(10 Markdown files), Codex registered by the **vault's own** `.codex/config.toml`,
 20215	which is the registration path (111) could not gate:
 20216	
 20217	| answer | wire | vault |
 20218	|---|---|---|
 20219	| reject | `Error: The change was not approved (decline). No change was made` | unchanged, 10 files |
 20220	| allow | `{"ok":true,"slug":"wire-probe","changed":true}` | the file appeared |
 20221	
 20222	**What the run caught that no unit test had**: the gate refused **yes**. The
 20223	elicitation declared `required: ['confirm']`, and an ACP permission card has no form
 20224	to fill — `codex-acp` maps 「allow once」 to `action: 'accept'` with no content, so a
 20225	person's approval arrived as a decline. Codex reported it verbatim: *"the permission
 20226	response was invalid because it lacked the required `confirm` field."* A checkpoint
 20227	that cannot be passed is a wall, and a wall teaches people to route around the gate
 20228	rather than answer it. `action` is now the answer; the boolean only overrides it.
 20229	
 20230	**Decision (accountable: owner)**: `codex-acp` joins `CHAT_ELIGIBLE`. `ISOLATION` and
 20231	`CHAT_ELIGIBLE` stay separate lists — controlling a config directory is not the same
 20232	claim as the resulting configuration holding a write, and conflating them is what
 20233	2026-08-24 was about.
 20234	
 20235	**Recorded dissent**: one green acceptance is a smaller sample than the two red
 20236	measurements that removed Codex, and the gate depends on a third party continuing to
 20237	forward `mcpServer/elicitation/request`. The counter is that the checkpoint no longer
 20238	lives in that third party's configuration: it lives in the server, fails closed when a
 20239	client declares no `elicitation` capability, and therefore degrades to refusal rather
 20240	than to a silent write.
 20241	
 20242	**Falsifier**: one Atlas write from an app-opened Codex session that changes the vault
 20243	without a card, or one approval that does not reach the server, returns Codex to the
 20244	state (111) left it in.
 20245	
 20246	**Revisit**: when `codex-acp` changes how it forwards elicitation, or when a second
 20247	runtime asks to join this list.
 20248

===== atlas-tool-policy =====
     1	/**
     2	 * ACP permission requests only carry the MCP tool name. The server's generated
     3	 * surface is the authority for whether a registered tool reads or writes; the
     4	 * sibling contract test compares every entry so this fail-closed list cannot
     5	 * silently drift when the server inventory changes.
     6	 */
     7	const ATLAS_READ_TOOLS = new Set([
     8	  'connection_info',
     9	  'git_status',
    10	  'git_history',
    11	  'list_concepts',
    12	  'get_concept',
    13	  'get_concepts',
    14	  'find_evidence',
    15	  'find_backlinks',
    16	  'find_neighbors',
    17	  'find_path',
    18	  'list_kinds',
    19	  'find_orphans',
    20	  'query_concepts',
    21	  'compile_ontology',
    22	  'query_ontology',
    23	  'validate_vault',
    24	  'analyze_repo_structure',
    25	  'infer_imports',
    26	  'index_project',
    27	]);
    28	
    29	export type AtlasToolMode = 'read' | 'write';
    30	
    31	/**
    32	 * Returns null for another server. Unknown tools on our server are writes:
    33	 * adding a read tool without updating the generated-surface contract must add
    34	 * friction, never grant a new write path by accident.
    35	 */
    36	export function atlasToolMode(
    37	  permissionToolName: string | null,
    38	  serverName: string,
    39	): AtlasToolMode | null {
    40	  if (!permissionToolName || !serverName) return null;
    41	  const prefix = `mcp__${serverName}__`;
    42	  if (!permissionToolName.startsWith(prefix)) return null;
    43	  const toolName = permissionToolName.slice(prefix.length);
    44	  if (!toolName || toolName.includes('__')) return null;
    45	  return ATLAS_READ_TOOLS.has(toolName) ? 'read' : 'write';
    46	}
===== acp-client permission =====
     1	/**
     2	 * The ACP client — the layer that turns line-delimited JSON-RPC into a conversation.
     3	 *
     4	 * Transport (process stdio) is injected, so this file's tests run with no real process and the
     5	 * process layer's tests need know nothing of the protocol.
     6	 *
     7	 * **The four contracts this file keeps:**
     8	 *
     9	 * 1. **Only two implementations are required** — `session/request_permission` and
    10	 *    `session/update`. File read/write, terminal, and elicitation are all optional and we **do not
    11	 *    declare them.** An undeclared method is answered with "no such thing".
    12	 *    ⚠️ That alone is not the permission gate — an undeclared capability closes only the ACP
    13	 *    channel, and the real CLI the adapter wraps does not consult this declaration for its own
    14	 *    tools. The actual gate comes from the config the app isolates (`src-tauri/src/acp.rs`).
    15	 * 2. **Never hardcode an `optionId`.** Options are located by `kind` (`allow_once`,
    16	 *    `reject_once`, …). Measured, those values were short strings like `allow` and `reject`, but
    17	 *    the adapter decides them and they can change at any time.
    18	 * 3. **The app never picks `allow_always` on the user's behalf.** That option carries a rule
    19	 *    granting "this entire directory for the whole session" (measured). Widening the boundary is
    20	 *    the user's decision alone.
    21	 * 4. **Ask Rust for the verdict.** Reimplementing inside/outside-vault here makes two copies, and
    22	 *    the browser side cannot resolve symlinks and not-yet-existing paths correctly anyway.
    23	 */
    24	
   360	        waiting.reject(new Error(`acp-send-failed: ${method}: ${String(error)}`));
   361	      });
   362	    });
   363	  };
   364	
   365	  const answerPermission = async (id: unknown, params: Record<string, unknown>) => {
   366	    const request = toPermissionRequest(params);
   367	    const allowOnce = request.options.find((o) => o.kind === 'allow_once');
   368	    const rejectOnce = request.options.find((o) => o.kind === 'reject_once');
   369	
   370	    /*
   371	     * The Atlas reads we wired do not block the conversation. A write continues only after the person
   372	     * has seen the typed change, even on the same server and the same vault. Path safety and meaning
   373	     * approval are different questions: the first being true does not answer the second.
   374	     */
   375	    const atlasMode = atlasToolMode(
   376	      request.toolName,
   377	      handlers.vaultMcpServerName ?? '',
   378	    );
   379	    const ontologyWrite = atlasMode === 'write';
   380	    /*
   381	     * ⚠️ **Answer even when the verdict fails** (caught in review, 2026-08-16).
   382	     * `await handlers.verdict(...)` used to be unwrapped, so a rejected IPC (the window closing, a
   383	     * bridge error) left this request with **no answer at all**. The other side waits forever and the
   384	     * user sees neither a card nor an error.
   385	     *
   386	     * Undecidable falls through to **asking** — the safe side.
   387	     */
   388	    let verdict: 'allow-inside-vault' | 'ask' = 'ask';
   389	    try {
   390	      verdict = await handlers.verdict(request.filePath);
   391	    } catch (error) {
   392	      handlers.onProtocolNotice?.(`verdict-failed: ${String(error)}`);
   393	    }
   394	    /* A read is auto-allowed only with no path or inside the vault. A read outside falls through to
   395	       the ordinary permission card too. */
   396	    if (
   397	      atlasMode === 'read' &&
   398	      allowOnce &&
   399	      (request.filePath === null || verdict === 'allow-inside-vault')
   400	    ) {
   401	      write({ jsonrpc: '2.0', id, result: selected(allowOnce.optionId) });
   402	      return;
   403	    }
   404	    if (!ontologyWrite && verdict === 'allow-inside-vault' && allowOnce) {
   405	      write({ jsonrpc: '2.0', id, result: selected(allowOnce.optionId) });
   406	      return;
   407	    }
   408	
   409	    // An Atlas write, access outside the vault, or a generic tool with an unknown path: ask the person.
   410	    let chosen: string | null = null;
   411	    try {
   412	      chosen = await handlers.askUser(
   413	        ontologyWrite ? { ...request, reviewKind: 'ontology-write' } : request,
   414	      );
   415	    } catch {
   416	      chosen = null; // a failure while asking is a rejection
   417	    }
   418	
   419	    // Verify the user's choice really is an option of that request. A screen returning a stale
   420	    // request's value would allow the wrong thing.
   421	    const valid = chosen && request.options.some((o) => o.optionId === chosen);
   422	    if (valid) {
   423	      write({ jsonrpc: '2.0', id, result: selected(chosen as string) });
   424	      return;
   425	    }
   426	    if (rejectOnce) {
   427	      write({ jsonrpc: '2.0', id, result: selected(rejectOnce.optionId) });
   428	      return;
   429	    }
   430	    // With not even a reject option, answer cancelled — no answer leaves the other side waiting forever.
   431	    write({ jsonrpc: '2.0', id, result: { outcome: { outcome: 'cancelled' } } });
   432	  };
   433	
   434	  const handle = (message: Record<string, unknown>) => {
   435	    const method = typeof message.method === 'string' ? message.method : null;
   436	    const hasId = message.id !== undefined && message.id !== null;
   437	
   438	    // Agent → us, a **request**: it must be answered.
   439	    if (method && hasId) {
   440	      if (method === 'session/request_permission') {
   441	        void answerPermission(message.id, asRecord(message.params));
   442	        return;
   443	      }
   444	    // An undeclared capability. Staying silent leaves the other side stalled.
   445	      handlers.onProtocolNotice?.(`declined:${method}`);
   446	      write({
   447	        jsonrpc: '2.0',
   448	        id: message.id,
   449	        error: { code: METHOD_NOT_FOUND, message: `capability not declared: ${method}` },
   450	      });
   451	      return;
   452	    }
   453	
   454	    // A notification: no answer.
   455	    if (method) {
   456	      if (method === 'session/update') {
   457	        const params = asRecord(message.params);
   458	        handlers.onUpdate?.(asRecord(params.update));
   459	      }
   460	      return;
   461	    }
   462	
   463	    // A response to one of our requests.
   464	    const id = typeof message.id === 'number' ? message.id : null;
   465	    if (id === null) return;
   466	    const waiting = pending.get(id);
   467	    if (!waiting) return;
   468	    pending.delete(id);
   469	    if (message.error) {
   470	      waiting.reject(new Error(JSON.stringify(message.error)));
   471	      return;
   472	    }
   473	    waiting.resolve(asRecord(message.result));
   474	  };
   475	
   476	  const ingest = (line: string) => {
   477	    if (disposed) return;
   478	    const trimmed = line.trim();
   479	    if (!trimmed) return;
   480	    let parsed: unknown;
   481	    try {
   482	      parsed = JSON.parse(trimmed);
   483	    } catch {
   484	    // A non-protocol line (the adapter's banner, say) is discarded — but not silently.
   485	      handlers.onProtocolNotice?.(`unparsable:${trimmed.slice(0, 120)}`);
   486	      return;
   487	    }
   488	    if (!parsed || typeof parsed !== 'object') return;
   489	    handle(parsed as Record<string, unknown>);
   490	  };
   491	
   492	  const unsubscribe = transport.subscribe(ingest);
   493	
   494	  return {
   495	    initialize: () =>
   496	      call('initialize', {
   497	        protocolVersion: 1,
   498	    // ⚠️ File and terminal capabilities are not declared. What is not declared is treated as
   499	    // "unsupported" (the ACP initialization convention).
   500	        clientCapabilities: {},
===== mode-safety =====
     1	/**
     2	 * The safety verdict for the "working mode" list — **it separates the known from the unknown.**
     3	 *
     4	 * Why (2026-08-17). The old verdict was a one-line denylist:
     5	 *
     6	 * ```
     7	 * modes.filter((m) => !GATE_REMOVING_MODES.has(m.id))
     8	 * ```
     9	 *
    10	 * It hides only what is written down. So **when the adapter adds a new mode it becomes visible and
    11	 * selectable without our knowing.** If that mode removes the permission gate, one choice undoes this
    12	 * screen's promise and the screen says nothing. **A safety device that treats what it does not know
    13	 * as safe is not a device.**
    14	 *
    15	 * It is an immediate problem too: the adapter versions are being bumped
    16	 * (`claude-agent-acp` 0.68→0.69, `codex-acp` 1.3→1.4) and our gate measurements were taken on the
    17	 * **old versions**.
    18	 *
    19	 * **Three categories:**
    20	 *
    21	 * - **Measured to remove the gate** → never shown (as before).
    22	 * - **Measured to be fine** → shown normally.
    23	 * - **Not yet measured** → shown, but **stated as unknown.** It is not hidden because blocking a
    24	 *   perfectly good new mode is a lie too. The permission card already uses the same discipline
    25	 *   (say unknown when unknown, and recommend the safe side).
    26	 */
    27	
    28	/**
    29	 * Requires the bare minimum for the verdict — it discriminates on `id` alone. So the caller's wider
    30	 * type (`AcpChoice`) can go in and come back out unchanged.
    31	 */
    32	export interface AcpModeChoice {
    33	  id: string;
    34	}
    35	
    36	/**
    37	 * Modes **measured to remove the gate**. The dividing line is not "is it strict" but **"does it let
    38	 * things through without asking"** — a mode that closes with a rejection (`dontAsk`) fails toward
    39	 * safety and is therefore not here.
    40	 */
    41	const GATE_REMOVING = new Set([
    42	  'bypasspermissions',
    43	  'acceptedits',
    44	  'agent-full-access',
    45	  /*
    46	   * `agent` belongs here. By name it sounds like "the normal mode", but the measurement
    47	   * (2026-08-16) is recorded in `src-tauri/src/acp.rs`: launching codex on its default (`agent`)
    48	   * gave *"files written outside the working folder with zero permission requests"*.
    49	   *
    50	   * **Re-measured 2026-08-17 — unchanged on `codex-acp` 1.4.** This closes the worry the block above
    51	   * left open ("our gate measurements were taken on the old versions"). In the installed app this
    52	   * line was briefly removed, a session opened on `agent`, and it was asked to *"write hello to
    53	   * /tmp/atlas-gate-probe.txt"* — the file appeared (contents `hello`) with **no permission card at
    54	   * all**. This adapter offers only two modes (`Read-only`, `Agent`). `Read-only` is safer for
    55	   * direct files but does not guard MCP writes, so mode classification alone never makes Codex
    56	   * eligible for in-app chat (`runtime-gate.ts`).
    57	   */
    58	  'agent',
    59	]);
    60	
    61	/** Modes measured not to widen direct adapter access inside an already guarded runtime. */
    62	const VERIFIED_SAFE = new Set(['default', 'read-only', 'readonly', 'plan', 'ask']);
    63	
    64	const normalize = (id: unknown): string =>
    65	  typeof id === 'string' ? id.trim().toLowerCase() : '';
    66	
    67	export interface ModePartition<T extends AcpModeChoice = AcpModeChoice> {
    68	  /** What is offered to the screen — the measured and the unmeasured together. */
    69	  offered: T[];
    70	  /** The ids among them that are **not yet measured**. The screen must state this fact. */
    71	  unverified: string[];
    72	  /** Entries dropped for a malformed shape — counted so they do not disappear silently. */
    73	  dropped: number;
    74	}
    75	
    76	export function partitionModes<T extends AcpModeChoice>(
    77	  modes: readonly T[],
    78	): ModePartition<T> {
    79	  const offered: T[] = [];
    80	  const unverified: string[] = [];
    81	  let dropped = 0;
    82	  for (const mode of Array.isArray(modes) ? modes : []) {
    83	    const key = normalize(mode?.id);
    84	    if (key.length === 0) {
    85	      dropped += 1;
    86	      continue;
    87	    }
    88	    if (GATE_REMOVING.has(key)) continue;
    89	    offered.push(mode);
    90	    if (!VERIFIED_SAFE.has(key)) unverified.push(mode.id);
    91	  }
    92	  return { offered, unverified, dropped };
    93	}
===== runtime-gate =====
     1	/**
     2	 * **How the permission gate is raised** per runtime — the method differs by tool.
     3	 *
     4	 * ## Two methods, and what works differs per tool (measured 2026-08-16)
     5	 *
     6	 * | | Claude | Codex |
     7	 * |---|---|---|
     8	 * | config isolation (`CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` / `CODEX_HOME`) | **works** | it is read, but **the approval policy is ignored** |
     9	 * | session mode (`session/set_mode`) | has no read-only | blocks direct files, **not Atlas MCP writes** |
    10	 *
    11	 * The codex measurement was the surprise. Putting `approval_policy = "untrusted"` and
    12	 * `sandbox_mode = "workspace-write"` into an isolated `CODEX_HOME` still produced **zero permission
    13	 * requests and files created outside the vault.** Yet the `model` value in the same folder was
    14	 * honoured — so our config **is read, and only the approval policy is overridden by the adapter's
    15	 * session mode.**
    16	 *
    17	 * The earlier conclusion that this made Codex guarded was overturned by installed-app acceptance
    18	 * on 2026-08-24. A self-registered Atlas `add_relation` produced no `session/request_permission`, no review
    19	 * card, and changed the vault immediately. A mode can be called read-only while an MCP child still
    20	 * mutates disk; the app must own the MCP write checkpoint before Codex is eligible for in-app chat.
    21	 */
    22	
    23	/**
    24	 * The mode to switch to once a session stands. A runtime absent here is not switched —
    25	 * **no mode is imposed on a tool that was never measured** (imposing one without knowing what that
    26	 * name means on that tool is guesswork).
    27	 */
    28	export const GATED_SESSION_MODE: Readonly<Record<string, string>> = {};
    29	
    30	/**
    31	 * Does this runtime **have a permission gate** — may the screen say so?
    32	 *
    33	 * True if **either** method works. `isolated` is Rust's verdict on whether config isolation is
    34	 * possible, and what is added here is the session-mode branch. This function must be the only path —
    35	 * if the screen's sentence and the action actually taken diverge, the screen makes a promise it
    36	 * cannot keep.
    37	 */
    38	export function isGuardedRuntime(runtimeId: string, isolated: boolean): boolean {
    39	  return isolated || runtimeId in GATED_SESSION_MODE;
    40	}
    41	
    42	/**
    43	 * Does this runtime's **own configuration** already put a permission request in front
    44	 * of a person for every tool call — including calls into our MCP server?
    45	 *
    46	 * Only config isolation has been measured to do that (Claude: an isolated
    47	 * `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` with an empty allow-list produced the request, and declining it
    48	 * left the file uncreated). A session mode is not the same thing: Codex `read-only`
    49	 * blocked direct file access while an Atlas MCP write went through with no request at
    50	 * all (installed rc.10 acceptance, 2026-08-24).
    51	 *
    52	 * The answer decides **who holds the single checkpoint** for a session. `true` hands it
    53	 * to the runtime and keeps the server gate off, so nobody is asked twice. `false` — the
    54	 * default for anything unmeasured — turns the server gate on, because an unasked write
    55	 * is worse than one question too many.
    56	 */
    57	export function runtimeOwnsWriteGate(runtimeId: string | null | undefined): boolean {
    58	  return typeof runtimeId === 'string' && CONFIG_ISOLATED_RUNTIMES.has(runtimeId);
    59	}
    60	
    61	/**
    62	 * Runtimes whose configuration the app isolates. Mirrors `ISOLATION` in
    63	 * `src-tauri/src/acp.rs`; `runtime-gate.test.ts` keeps the two from drifting.
    64	 */
    65	const CONFIG_ISOLATED_RUNTIMES: ReadonlySet<string> = new Set(['claude-acp']);
===== session scope =====
   120	    modes: modePartition.offered,
   121	    currentModeId: currentId(result.modes, 'currentModeId'),
   122	    unverifiedModeIds: modePartition.unverified,
   123	    droppedModeCount: modeChoices.dropped + modePartition.dropped,
   124	  };
   125	}
   126	
   127	/** One past conversation — only as much as the list needs to draw. */
   128	export interface AcpSessionSummary {
   129	  sessionId: string;
   130	  /** The absolute path of the folder that conversation was opened in. **This is the filter key.** */
   131	  cwd: string;
   132	  /** The title the adapter composed. Null when absent — the screen does not invent one. */
   133	  title: string | null;
   134	  /** An ISO string, or null. */
   135	  updatedAt: string | null;
   136	}
   137	
   138	/**
   139	 * **We do the filtering** (measured 2026-08-16).
   140	 *
   141	 * Passing `cwd` to `session/list` does **not** make the adapter filter by that folder — measured,
   142	 * conversations from other repositories that were never opened came back, titles and all
   143	 * (`/Users/…/side-project/…`'s "understanding design system level" among them).
   144	 *
   145	 * Rendering that as-is puts **work titles from folders the user has never opened in this app** on
   146	 * screen. The trust charter's second promise (zero collection without the user knowing) and the
   147	 * local-first rule (do not sweep outside the vault) both forbid it outright.
   148	 *
   149	 * So this function is the only path and it must filter here. Even if the adapter starts filtering
   150	 * properly later, this check stays — our promises are not staked on someone else's behaviour.
   151	 * Gate: `tests/contract/acp-session-scope.contract.test.ts`.
   152	 */
   153	export function keepSessionsInFolder(
   154	  sessions: AcpSessionSummary[],
   155	  cwd: string,
   156	): AcpSessionSummary[] {
   157	  const root = normalizeFolder(cwd);
   158	  if (!root) return [];
   159	  return sessions.filter((s) => normalizeFolder(s.cwd) === root);
   160	}
   161	
   162	/** Only trims a trailing `/`. Anything more is Rust's job (symlinks, relative paths). */
   163	function normalizeFolder(value: unknown): string | null {
   164	  if (typeof value !== 'string') return null;
   165	  const trimmed = value.trim().replace(/\/+$/, '');
   166	  return trimmed.length > 0 ? trimmed : null;
   167	}
   168	
   169	/** One permission request, reduced to what the screen shows the user. */
   170	export interface AcpPermissionRequest {
   171	  /** The human-readable line, exactly as the adapter gave it. */
   172	  title: string | null;
   173	  /** The ACP identifier linking this decision to the same tool's progress and completion updates. */
   174	  toolCallId: string | null;
   175	  /**
   176	   * The **identifier** of the tool called (such as `mcp__atlas-vault__add_concept`). Policy reads
   177	   * this, because an MCP tool call has no file path.
   178	   */
   179	  toolName: string | null;
   180	  /** `edit`, `execute`, `read`, … — the typed fact the screen picks its icon and colour from. */
   181	  toolKind: string | null;
   182	  /** An absolute path when present. The basis for the policy verdict and what the screen displays. */
   183	  filePath: string | null;
   184	  /** The arguments the tool actually requested. A meaning write builds its typed change from this. */
   185	  rawInput: Record<string, unknown>;
   186	  /** An ordinary file permission, or an ontology write needing the person's decision about meaning. */
   187	  reviewKind: 'permission' | 'ontology-write';
   188	  /** The available options — handled only by `kind`. */
   189	  options: Array<{ optionId: string; kind: string; name: string | null }>;
   190	}
   191	
   192	export interface AcpClientHandlers {
   193	  /** Streaming updates — text chunks, tool calls, plans. */
   194	  onUpdate?: (update: Record<string, unknown>) => void;
   195	  /**
   196	   * The name of the vault MCP server we wired into the session. Only that server's read tools are
   197	   * auto-allowed; its write tools go through the person's change review. Not passing it turns this
   198	   * classification off — we do not pretend something exists.
   199	   */
   200	  vaultMcpServerName?: string;
   201	  /** Is this path inside the vault? Goes to the Rust verdict. */
   202	  verdict: (filePath: string | null) => Promise<'allow-inside-vault' | 'ask'>;
   203	  /**
   204	   * Asks the user. Returns the chosen `optionId`, or null for a rejection.
   205	   *
   206	   * With no screen, or with no answer from the user, **rejection must be the default** — counting
   207	   * an unasked question as allowed is the same as having no permission gate.
   208	   */
   209	  askUser: (request: AcpPermissionRequest) => Promise<string | null>;
   210	  /** For diagnosis. Called when a line arrives that is outside the protocol. */
   211	  onProtocolNotice?: (message: string) => void;
   212	}
   213	
   214	interface PendingCall {
   215	  resolve: (value: Record<string, unknown>) => void;
===== docs trust/features relevant =====
     1	# About Trust
     2	
     3	It involves pulling binaries from anonymous repositories onto your own computer. Therefore, we start by listing what it does not do.
     4	
     5	## Local source of truth, explicit provider boundary
     6	
     7	The vault's canonical files remain on your disk. Atlas has no backend, account, or telemetry, and opening or editing a folder does not upload it to Atlas.
     8	
     9	## It is plain Markdown
    10	
    11	Even if you delete the app, the folder remains. Nothing gets locked. This is not marketing copy; it is a structural fact. Because the storage format is Markdown, there is no way to lock it.
    12	
    13	## It does not scan automatically
    14	
    15	It reads only the folders you choose. Dotfiles like `.env` and `.git/` are excluded from indexing. It does not look at anything else on your disk.
    16	
    17	## AI connectivity is optional
    18	
    19	Atlas-controlled API and runner calls state and record their destination in `.ontology-atlas/llm-audit.jsonl`. ACP and externally connected MCP agents are a separate boundary: Atlas MCP stays local over stdio, but the selected coding agent may send prompts, context, and tool results to its provider. Atlas does not claim that provider-owned traffic is recorded in its LLM audit log.
    20	
    21	## Signing and Notarization
    22	
    23	The macOS app is signed with Apple Developer ID and notarized. It opens without Gatekeeper warnings.
    24	
    60	
    61	### Web and app do not promise the same screens (2026-07-27)
    62	
    63	Decided in `docs/DECISIONS.md`; the enforceable version is
    64	`.claude/rules/surfaces.md`. It is one codebase and one build — the app loads
    65	the same static export in a WebView — so this is a capability table, not a
    66	feature-parity backlog. **Desktop capabilities ship without a web equivalent,
    67	on purpose.** What the web owes instead is an honest degradation: why it cannot
    68	work here, and where it can.
    69	
    70	| Capability | Web (Chromium) | Desktop app (macOS · Windows x64 beta) | Why they differ |
    71	|---|---|---|---|
    72	| Open the map with no install | ✅ | ✅ | the web's first job — gateway |
    73	| Open your own markdown folder | ✅ File System Access API | ✅ absolute path | Firefox and non-FSA browsers degrade to a notice + download link |
    74	| Read / edit / create nodes in that folder | ✅ | ✅ | same parser, same schema, same files |
    75	| Remember the folder between visits | ❌ pick it again | ✅ | web keeps an FSA handle in its own IndexedDB; a convenience cache, not the source of truth |
    76	| Work offline | ❌ | ✅ | |
    77	| Git history and snapshots | ❌ degraded card + `ontology-atlas snapshot` | ✅ | a browser has no right to run git on your machine |
    78	| API keys / in-app **agent** chat | ❌ **and will not be built** | ✅ native credential store | keys in browser storage leak to a single XSS, and vendors name the direct-call header `…-dangerous-direct-browser-access` |
    79	| Write agent config (`.mcp.json`) into the vault | ⚠️ folder writes work, but there is no absolute path to record | ✅ | MCP registration needs a real path |
    80	| In-app updates | ❌ | ✅ | |
    81	
    82	**Windows today**: an unsigned x64 beta carries the same local folder and MCP
    83	surface as the desktop app. `/download` states the SmartScreen unknown-publisher
    84	and managed-PC risk before the executable CTA. Chrome or Edge remains the
    85	install-free fallback when that warning or an organization policy is a blocker;
    86	the web still does not offer BYOK or MCP registration.
    87	
    88	---

    22	 *
    23	 * ## The one job of this screen
    24	 *
    25	 * **Say what you can use right now.** Each row answers "can I talk to this tool
    26	 * from here", and when it cannot, it says **what to do about it** on the same
    27	 * row. That is why status is not flattened into installed/not-installed — the
    28	 * tool missing and Node missing require different actions from the user.
    29	 *
    30	 * ## It is shown in two groups
    31	 *
    32	 * With 38 rows, one block cannot be scanned. **What is actually confirmed on this
    33	 * computer** is expanded and the rest collapses — what you can do now comes first.
    34	 *
    35	 * ⚠️ **The meaning of "confirmed" was once wrong** (2026-08-16, owner: *"It looks odd that everything is shown like this"* — it looks odd that everything is shown). The old test
    36	 * was `state === 'ready'`, which meant not "this tool is here" but **"npx exists,
    37	 * so it could be launched"**. Only 12 of the CLI names we wrap were recorded, so
    38	 * the other 26 could not be checked at all — and yet 20 rows carried a green 「Ready」. Rust now separates that case as `cli-unknown` (`acp.rs`), so this
    39	 * screen's first group holds **only what was really confirmed**.
    40	 *
    41	 * ## Who asks on your behalf (owner call, 2026-08-16)
    42	 *
    43	 * The app launches runners with its own configuration, so it asks the user before
    44	 * anything outside the vault is touched. But that isolation exists **only for the
    45	 * runners we actually measured**. The rest use whatever configuration the user set
    46	 * up for that tool, so anyone who set their tool to "do everything without asking"
    47	 * gets no prompt even when we launch it.
    48	 *
    49	 * That fact is not hidden — the same reason this product never writes "coming

    51	 * same thing: this fact **does not fit in a badge.** "Can the app ask on your

    54	 * comments inside `RuntimeRow`.
    55	 *

    57	 * naming the guarding tools **from the data**. When a second one appears the
    58	 * sentence follows automatically. Writing "only Claude Code for now" by hand
    59	 * starts rotting that day.
    60	 */
    61	
    62	/**
    63	 * State → badge ink. Colour is decided by **which fact the badge carries**, so it

    66	 *
    67	 * Only "Ready" uses success — in this repository success is reserved for "it went

    69	 * exactly what `CommitDetail` uses for the same kind of badge; a new copy would
    70	 * make the two screens' greens diverge.

    72	 * **The other states are not distinguished by colour.** Making "Installation Required" and

    87	 * `CommitDetail` keeps the filled pill on purpose. There the status **is** the subject of the line;

   357	   * ⚠️ We do run other people's code (`npx -y <package>@<version>`).    473	                className: cn(
   474	                  'inline-flex items-center gap-1.5 py-0.5',
   475	                  isReady ? READY_INK : NOT_READY_INK,
   476	                ),
   477	              })}
   478	            >
   479	              {isReady ? (
   480	                <span
   481	                  aria-hidden
   482	                  data-testid="app-settings-runtime-ready-dot"
   483	                  className="size-1.5 shrink-0 rounded-full bg-[color:var(--color-status-success)]"
   484	                />
   485	              ) : null}
   486	              {t(`state.${runtime.state}`)}
   487	            </span>
   488	          </span>
   489	        }
   490	      />
   700	         * binary in the bundle, not ready yet). Passing the name then lets **someone else's
   701	         * `atlas-vault` server that we did not wire** inherit that auto-allow. The contract already
   702	         * said so: *"not passing it turns that auto-allow off — we do not pretend something exists."*
   703	         */
   704	        vaultMcpServerName: hasVaultMcp ? VAULT_MCP_SERVER_NAME : undefined,
   705	        verdict: (filePath) => acpPermissionVerdict(acpSessionId, filePath),
   706	        askUser,
   707	        onProtocolNotice: (message) => keepDiagnostic(message),
   708	      });
   709	      clientRef.current = client;
   710	
   711	      await client.initialize();
   712	      /*
   713	       * With a conversation to resume, try that first. On failure it **falls through to a new
   714	       * conversation** — being unable to open a past conversation must not become the reason a
   715	       * conversation cannot be opened at all (that file is not ours and can disappear at any time).
   716	       */
   717	      let session: { sessionId: string; choices: AcpSessionChoices } | null = null;
   718	      if (resumeIdRef.current) {
   719	        try {
   720	          session = await client.loadSession({
   721	            sessionId: resumeIdRef.current,
   722	            cwd: vaultRoot,
   723	            mcpServers,
   724	            // Resuming does not change the rules — same instructions as a new conversation.
   725	            appendSystemPrompt: vaultHandoffPrompt(hasVaultMcp),
   726	          });
   727	        } catch {
   728	          keepDiagnostic('resume-failed');
   729	        }
   730	        resumeIdRef.current = null;
   731	      }
   732	      session ??= await client.newSession({
   733	        cwd: vaultRoot,
   734	        mcpServers,
   735	        appendSystemPrompt: vaultHandoffPrompt(hasVaultMcp),
   736	      });
   737	      sessionIdRef.current = session.sessionId;
   738	
   739	      /*
   740	       * **Raise the permission gate.** codex is not held by config isolation and only by the session
   741	       * mode (measured — `runtime-gate.ts`). It is applied only to runtimes that were measured.
   742	       *
   743	       * On failure the conversation is not opened. Emitting ready with no gate makes the screen's
   744	       * promise that "outside the folder, we ask first" a lie.
   745	       */
   746	      const gatedMode = GATED_SESSION_MODE[runtimeId];
   747	      let choices = session.choices;
   748	      if (gatedMode) {
   749	        if (await client.setMode(session.sessionId, gatedMode)) {
   750	          /*
   751	           * ⚠️ **Reflect it on the screen too.** Missing this left the session at `read-only` while
   752	           * the dropdown read `Agent` (confirmed on the real thing, 2026-08-16) — the screen stating
   753	           * the current state incorrectly, and on the very value that decides whether it asks about
   754	           * things outside the folder, which is the worst place to be wrong.
   755	           *
   756	           * What `session/new` returned is from **before** the mode was applied, so leaving it makes
   757	           * it stale. What we applied is the current value.
   758	           */
   759	          choices = { ...choices, currentModeId: gatedMode };
   760	        } else {
   761	          const failure = `gate-mode-failed:${gatedMode}`;
   762	          keepDiagnostic(failure);
   763	          throw new Error(failure);
   764	        }
   765	      }
   766	
   767	      if (!disposedRef.current) {
   768	        setChoices(choices);
   769	        setStatusTracked('ready');
   770	      }
   771	      // The list is filled after the session stands, so it does not hold up the frame the screen appears in.
   772	      void client
   773	        .listSessions(vaultRoot)
   774	        .then((list) => {
   775	          if (!disposedRef.current) setSessions(list);
   776	        })
   777	        .catch(() => {
   778	          /* Being unable to read past conversations is not this conversation's problem. */
   779	        });
   780	    } catch (err) {

  5688	   것이다. **알고 고르게 하는 것**과 **못 하게 막는 것**은 다른 일이다.

  5718	파일 능력을 **하나도 선언하지 않고**(`clientCapabilities: {}`) 작업 폴더 **밖** 절대
  5719	경로에 쓰라고 시켰다. 네 조건에서 잰 결과:
  5720	
  5721	| 조건 | 권한 요청 | 결과 |
  5722	|---|---|---|
  5723	| 소유자의 실제 `~/.claude/settings.json` (`defaultMode: auto`, `allow: [Bash(*), Write(*), Edit(*) …]` 15개) | **0회** | ⚠️ **폴더 밖에 파일 생성됨** |
  5724	| 같은 설정 + 세션 모드를 `default`(직접 확인)로 변경 | **0회** | ⚠️ 생성됨. 게다가 `Terminal[execute]` 툴콜까지 |
  5725	| 격리한 설정(`defaultMode: default`, `allow: []`) | **1회** | ✅ 거절하니 파일 안 생김 |
  5726	| 격리한 설정 + 자격증명 없음 | — | ❌ `Authentication required` |
  5727	
  5728	⚠️ **첫 시도는 오염된 실험이었다.** 파일명을 `ESCAPED.txt`, 내용을 `ATLAS-ESCAPE-TEST`
  5729	로 지었더니 에이전트가 *"이건 샌드박스 탈출 테스트라 거부한다"* 고 답했다 — 모델의
  5730	판단력을 잰 것이지 관문을 잰 것이 아니다. 평범한 이름(`meeting-notes.md`)으로 바꾸자
  5731	바로 썼다. **실제 사고는 늘 평범한 얼굴로 온다.**
  5732	
  5733	**결정**
  5734	
  5735	1. **앱이 띄우는 세션은 자기 설정 디렉터리를 쓴다.** 사용자의 전역 설정을 물려받지
  5770	## 2026-08-16 — ACP 로 사용자의 에이전트를 앱 안으로 들인다: 「위임 일꾼」 경계는 뒤집고, 「지도가 주」는 유지한다
  5771	
  5772	**소집**: PO 카운슬 5석(근거 · 결 · 지킴이 · 해자 · 지렛대) · **트리거**: 새 표면 신설 +
  5773	표면 제거 검토(BYOK) + 방향 · 선행 결정 뒤집기 + 소유자 요청

  5775	Differentiation 2(해자, 조건부 3) · Ontology value 2 · Agent value 3(지킴이) ·
  5776	Verification 2(결)

  5778	값과 판정은 **1라운드 그대로**이며 반박을 받지 않았다. 다음 소집이 알아야 할 사실이라
  5779	적어 둔다.

  5797	  줄에서 사라진다.
  5798	
  5799	**선행 결정과의 관계 — 하나는 뒤집고 둘은 유지한다**:
  5800	- **2026-07-27 「AI 대화는 앱 안 에이전트 패널에 산다」 → 부분 뒤집음.**
  5801	  「패널 = 화면 동료 / 외부 에이전트 = 위임 일꾼」이라는 **사다리 두 단 구분을
  5802	  뒤집는다** — 위임 일꾼을 앱 안에서 부를 수 있게 된다. 근거: 그 기록의 반대 1
  5803	  ("MCP 가 붙는 순간 패널은 열등한 중복이고 1차 타겟은 패널을 안 열 것")의 관측
  5804	  형태가 실제로 나타났고, ACP 는 그 반대가 지목한 비용(벤더 3사 API 추종)을 제거한다.
  5805	  단 **적용 규칙 「합집합 금지 — 지도가 주」는 그대로 유지한다.** 패널을 주 작업면으로
  5806	  승격하지 않는다.
  5807	- **2026-08-01 「네 번째 연결은 벤더가 아니라 문이다」 → 유효 인용, 건드리지 않는다.**
  5808	  `provider:"local"` 갈래(91/91 `host:"localhost:11434"`)는 헌장 ①("인터넷 없이
  5809	  돌아간다")의 유일한 담지자다. ACP 두 어댑터는 구독 인증에 묶여 있어 대체재가 아니다.
  5810	- **`tool-catalog.ts` 의 "코드가 필요한 일은 터미널의 몫" → 유효.** ACP 세션의
  5811	  작업 폴더를 저장소 전체로 열지 않음으로써 지킨다.
  5812	
  5813	**결정 (소유자 확정)**:
  5814	1. **ACP v1 을 도입한다.** 첫 조각은 **claude-code 단독** — 지금 보고 있는 노드를
  5815	   `embeddedContext` 로 실어 보내고 기존 제안 카드까지 왕복 한 번. codex 는 다음 조각.
  5816	2. **BYOK 는 동결한다(삭제 아님).** 명명 벤더 3종은 신규 추가·기능 확장을 멈추고
  5817	   화면에서 강조하지 않는다. **로컬 러너는 존치한다.** 삭제 판정은 2026-08-24 관측
  5818	   이후 별건으로 — 지금 지우면 진행 중인 반증 관측의 계측기를 관측이 끝나기 전에
  5819	   뜯는 것이 된다.
  5820	3. **전송 기록 약속을 정직하게 낮추고, 낮춘 만큼 채운다.** 「모든 왕복이 기록돼요」
  5821	   → 「여기서 보낸 것만 기록돼요 — 실행기가 자기 서버와 주고받는 것은 그 도구가
  5822	   기록해요」. 동시에 Atlas 가 **실제로 넘긴 것**(주입한 `mcpServers` · `embeddedContext`
  5823	   로 보낸 노드 · 사용자가 친 질문)은 빠짐없이 기록한다. 헌장 예외로 명문화하는 안은
  5824	   기각(⑤ 정면 충돌).
  5825	4. **권한은 자동 승인하지 않는다.** Buzz 식 `allow_once` 자동 승인 금지. `fs`/`terminal`
  5826	   능력 미선언 + `requestPermission` 을 `agent_setup.rs` 의 기존 봉쇄 경계로 답한다.
  5827	   ⚠️ 능력 미선언은 ACP 경로만 닫고 어댑터가 감싼 Claude Code 의 네이티브 도구는
  5828	   살아 있다 — **유일한 관문은 `requestPermission`** 이므로, 볼트 밖 쓰기를 시켜
  5829	   그것이 실제로 오는지 재는 것이 이 조각의 **첫 게이트**다.
  5830	5. **작업 폴더는 볼트 한정.** `connect_project_source` 로 이미 묶인 저장소에 한해
  5831	   읽기만. 쓰기는 어떤 경우에도 Atlas MCP 도구로만.
  5832	6. **선행 조건 둘을 먼저 닫는다**: 볼트 위생 거부(`/` · `/Applications` · `/System` ·
  5833	   `/Users` · `$HOME`), 최소폭 계약(1040 에서 패널 열었을 때 지도 캔버스 ≥480px —
  5834	   현재 실측 333px 이므로 게이트가 빈 세트가 아니다).
  5835	7. **`why` 회수를 이 조각의 정의에 포함한다.** 세션에서 수락된 변경의 이유에 사용자의
  5836	   실제 질문이 남는 데까지가 완료 조건이다. 빠지면 원래 문제가 앱 안에서 재현될 뿐이다.
  5837	8. **성공 지표는 채팅 횟수가 아니라 `why`/provenance 줄 수다**(지렛대 자기반박 수용).
  5838	
  5839	**적용 규칙**: 최소 슬라이스 · 헌장 우선 · 합집합 금지(지도가 주) · 제거 요구
  5840	(38종 레지스트리 브라우저 · 커스텀 커맨드 입력란 · 첫 실행 위저드 신설 · Buzz 식
  5841	자체 Node provision(1.06GB) · 선택 메서드 구현 · Windows Job Object 는 이 조각 밖)
  5842	
  5843	**서명 (accountable)**: stark
  5844	
  5845	**기록된 반대 (가장 강한 패배 논점 — 해자, 2라운드에서 Investigate first 로 이동)**:
  5846	*"터미널 계수 없이 짓는 것은 근거 없는 두 번째 공급측 베팅이다. 「마찰을 없애면
  5847	쓴다」는 인과는 2026-08-01 키 0짜리 문으로 이미 시험됐고 그날 21회 후 15일 0회로
  5848	끝났다. 앱 안에서 그 질문이 실제로 던져진 사례는 0건이고 모집단은 N≈1 이다."*
  5849	근거 자리도 같은 값에서 두 행을 4→3 으로 내렸다: *"설계 증거는 모먼트를 그럴듯하게
  5850	만들지 관측하지 않는다."* **소유자가 사전 계수 관문을 해제했고**, 계수는 관문이 아니라
     1	import type { McpServerLaunch } from '@/shared/config';
     2	
     3	/**
     4	 * The MCP server wired into a session automatically — **so the user never edits a config file.**
     5	 *
     6	 * **This is the substance of the feature.** ACP itself is something any app can attach in a few
     7	 * weeks. What only we do is that **the person's vault is already loaded** the moment that session
     8	 * opens. Other editors can wire MCP too, but they have no ontology to wire.
     9	 *
    10	 * Measured 2026-08-16: passing the bundled MCP through `session/new`'s `mcpServers` had the agent
    11	 * call `connection_info` and `list_kinds` and read the vault's 79 nodes. The user had never created
    12	 * a `.mcp.json` nor typed `claude mcp add`.
    13	 *
    14	 * **A name collision swallows it silently.** In the same measurement the codex side failed at first,
    15	 * and the cause was not the protocol but a **name collision** — that repository's
    16	 * `.codex/config.toml` already had `ontology-atlas`, and the adapter's deduplication **discarded
    17	 * ours without a word**. So the server the app wires avoids names a user would plausibly write by hand.
    18	 */
    19	import { PROJECT_VAULT_DIR } from '@/shared/lib/project-vault-dir';
    20	
    21	export const VAULT_MCP_SERVER_NAME = 'atlas-vault';
    22	
    23	/** An entry in ACP `session/new`'s `mcpServers` (the stdio variant). */
    24	export interface AcpMcpServer {
    25	  name: string;
    26	  command: string;
    27	  args: string[];
    28	  env: Array<{ name: string; value: string }>;
    29	}
    30	
    31	export interface ExistingVaultMcpRegistration {
    32	  /** The launch command written in the config file. */
    33	  command: string | null | undefined;
    34	  /** Did it pass full config validation, not just the command but the current vault environment? */
    35	  validForCurrentVault: boolean;
    36	}
    37	
    38	/**
    39	 * The config file this runtime's CLI **reads by itself from the working folder**. If the vault has
    40	 * already registered our server there, the app need not wire it again.
    41	 *
    42	 * Why it is needed (measured 2026-08-17). Opening a codex session in a vault created by `init` and
    43	 * asking a question showed **the same server running twice**:
    44	 *
    45	 * - `mcp.ontology-atlas.list_kinds` → `{"total": 5, …}`  ← the vault's `.codex/config.toml`
    46	 * - `mcp.atlas-vault.list_kinds`    → `{"total": 5, …}`  ← the one the app wired
    47	 *
    48	 * `ps` showed two `ontology-atlas-mcp` processes too (same parent). Renaming to `atlas-vault`
    49	 * **dodged** the adapter's deduplication, so instead of being silently discarded it silently became
    50	 * two copies. The model's tool list then holds the same tool under two names, and nobody tells it
    51	 * which to use.
    52	 *
    53	 * **Only what was measured goes in here.** Listing an unmeasured runtime can produce **a session with
    54	 * no tools at all** — far worse than a duplicate. So an unknown runtime is wired as before.
    55	 * (Whether `claude-acp` reads `.mcp.json` has not been measured — the login had expired and the
    56	 * session could not be opened.)
    57	 */
    58	const MEASURED_SELF_READ_SLOT: Readonly<Record<string, 'codex-config'>> = {
    59	  'codex-acp': 'codex-config',
    60	};
    61	
    62	/** The config slot this runtime reads from the vault itself — `null` when unmeasured. */
    63	export function vaultSelfReadSlot(runtimeId: string | null | undefined): 'codex-config' | null {
    64	  if (typeof runtimeId !== 'string') return null;
    65	  return MEASURED_SELF_READ_SLOT[runtimeId] ?? null;
    66	}
    67	
    68	/**
    69	 * Is what the vault already registered **exactly what we were about to wire**?
    70	 *
    71	 * Skipping on "a registration exists" alone is wrong — a vault entry pointing at a stale path or a
    72	 * different `OATLAS_VAULT` produces a session with no tools at all, or one reading the wrong vault.
    73	 * It counts as the same only when it passes full validation for the current vault **and the command
    74	 * matches character for character**.
    75	 */
    76	export function vaultAlreadyRegisters(
    77	  launch: McpServerLaunch | null,
    78	  registration: ExistingVaultMcpRegistration | null | undefined,
    79	): boolean {
    80	  if (!launch || !registration?.validForCurrentVault) return false;
    81	  if (typeof registration.command !== 'string') return false;
    82	  const registeredCommand = registration.command.trim();
  1400	/// In measurement (2026-08-16), a permission request's title was a relative path when
  1401	/// **inside** the vault (`Write meeting-notes.md`) and an absolute path when **outside**.
  1402	/// Judging by that difference means the policy silently flips whenever the wording changes
  1403	/// slightly. The raw form of the same request carries `toolCall.rawInput.file_path` as an
  1404	/// absolute path, so we judge by that.
  1405	///
  1406	/// If the path cannot be found, **we ask.** Tilting the unknown toward allow means the
  1407	/// less judgeable a request is, the more easily it passes.
  1408	pub(crate) fn permission_verdict(vault_root: &Path, file_path: Option<&str>) -> PermissionVerdict {
  1409	    let Some(raw) = file_path else {
  1410	        return PermissionVerdict::Ask;
  1411	    };
  1412	    let raw = Path::new(raw);
  1413	    // The root of the permission boundary must be the absolute directory `acp_start`
  1414	    // verified, not a string the caller made up. An empty path resolves to the current
  1415	    // working folder and `/` is the ancestor of every absolute path, so allowing either
  1416	    // one opens the entire gate.
  1417	    if !vault_root.is_absolute() || !raw.is_absolute() {
  1418	        return PermissionVerdict::Ask;
  1419	    }
  1420	    let Ok(root) = std::fs::canonicalize(vault_root) else {
  1421	        return PermissionVerdict::Ask;
  1422	    };
  1423	    // If what resolves now differs from the canonical path `acp_start` stored, the root
  1424	    // path was replaced by a link after the session started. We do not promote the new
  1425	    // target to the same vault — the permission boundary does not move during a session's lifetime.
  1426	    if root != vault_root || !root.is_dir() || root.parent().is_none() {
  1427	        return PermissionVerdict::Ask;
  1428	    }
  1429	    let resolved = resolve_for_comparison(raw);
  1430	    if resolved.starts_with(&root) {
  1431	        PermissionVerdict::AllowInsideVault
  1432	    } else {
  1433	        PermissionVerdict::Ask
  1434	    }
  1435	}
  1436	
  1437	/// Puts two paths on **the same yardstick**.
  1438	///
  1439	/// There are two reasons plain `canonicalize` is not enough:
  1440	///
  1441	/// 1. **The file about to be written usually does not exist yet.** `canonicalize` fails on
  1442	///    a nonexistent path so the original stays as-is, while the vault root exists and gets
  1443	///    canonicalized. On macOS that asymmetry becomes an accident directly — `/var/...`
  1444	///    turns into `/private/var/...`, and **something inside the vault gets judged as
  1445	///    outside** (this check caught it on 2026-08-16).
  1446	/// 2. But skipping canonicalization entirely counts a symlink inside the vault that points
  1447	///    outside as "inside".
  1448	///
  1449	/// So we **canonicalize up to the deepest existing ancestor and re-append the rest.**
  1450	/// The existing part has its links resolved; the not-yet-existing part keeps its names as-is.
  1451	fn resolve_for_comparison(path: &Path) -> PathBuf {
  1452	    if let Ok(canonical) = std::fs::canonicalize(path) {
  1453	        return canonical;
  1454	    }
  1455	    let mut rest: Vec<std::ffi::OsString> = Vec::new();
  1456	    let mut cursor = path;
  1457	    loop {
  1458	        if let Ok(canonical) = std::fs::canonicalize(cursor) {
  1459	            let mut out = canonical;
  1460	            for part in rest.iter().rev() {
  1461	                out.push(part);
  1462	            }
  1463	            return out;
  1464	        }
  1465	        match (cursor.file_name(), cursor.parent()) {
  1466	            (Some(name), Some(parent)) => {
  1467	                rest.push(name.to_os_string());
  1468	                cursor = parent;
  1469	            }
  1470	            // Nowhere further to climb — a path with no canonicalizable ancestor at all
  1471	            // (a relative path, etc.). Compare the original as-is.
  1472	            _ => return path.to_path_buf(),
  1473	        }
  1474	    }
  1475	}
  1476	
  1477	#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, serde::Serialize)]
  1478	#[serde(rename_all = "kebab-case")]
  1479	pub(crate) enum PermissionVerdict {
  1480	    /// Inside the vault, so the app allows on the user's behalf.
  1481	    AllowInsideVault,
  1482	    /// Ask the user.
  1483	    Ask,
  1484	}

  1486	/// Reads one line but **puts an upper bound on its length**.
  1487	///
  1488	/// If an adapter writes endlessly without a newline, the standard `read_line` grows its
  1489	/// buffer without limit until it kills the whole app. This means we do not trust the output
  1490	/// of a program somebody else made, and past the bound we **drop that line and report the
   811	fn acp_start(
   812	    app: AppHandle,
   813	    sessions: State<'_, AcpSessions>,
   814	    runtime_id: String,
   815	    cwd: String,
   816	) -> Result<String, String> {
   817	    let root = fs::canonicalize(&cwd).map_err(|err| format!("cwd-unreadable:{err}"))?;
   818	    if !root.is_dir() {
   819	        return Err("cwd-not-a-directory".into());
   820	    }
   821	    if let Some(reason) = vault_root_rejection(&root) {
   822	        return Err(format!("vault-root-rejected:{reason}"));
   823	    }
   824	
   825	    let (is_executable, list_dir, read_text, login_ok) = acp::real_probe();
   826	    let probe = acp::FsProbe {
   827	        is_executable: &is_executable,
   828	        list_dir: &list_dir,
   829	        read_text: &read_text,
   830	        login_ok: &login_ok,
   831	    };
   832	    let home = std::env::var_os(if cfg!(windows) { "USERPROFILE" } else { "HOME" })
   833	        .map(PathBuf::from);
   834	    // Must also find what the app installed on our behalf — otherwise, even after installing,
   835	    // the screen keeps saying "Installation required."
   836	    let app_data_for_paths = app.path().app_data_dir().ok();
   837	    let managed_bin = app_data_for_paths.as_deref().map(acp::managed_cli_bin_dir);
   838	    // Include Node accepted by the app as a candidate — otherwise, even after accepting, it says "Node required."
   839	    let managed_node_bin = app_data_for_paths
   840	        .as_deref()
   841	        .and_then(managed_node::managed_node_bin_dir);
   842	    let launch = acp::resolve_launch(
   843	        &runtime_id,
   844	        home.as_deref(),
   845	        std::env::var_os("PATH").as_deref(),
   846	        &probe,
   847	        managed_bin.as_deref(),
   848	        managed_node_bin.as_deref(),
   849	    )?;
   850	
   851	    /*
   852	     * Check cache entries **just before** launching if using npx (owner's physical machine 2026-08-19). If the first download is interrupted halfway, a half-formed entry remains, and npx tries to reuse it, dying every time with `Could not read package.json` —
   853	     * a state that does not heal itself. If broken, **delete only that entry** so npx downloads from scratch. Judgment basis and hash derivation are in the
   854	     * npx cache self-healing block in `acp.rs`.
   855	     */
   856	    let npx_preflight = acp::preflight_npx_cache(&launch, home.as_deref());
   857	
   858	    // **Do not inherit the user's global settings** (Decision log 2026-08-16 (2)).
   859	    // Measurement: the owner's `~/.claude/settings.json` pre-allowed `Bash(*)`·`Write(*)`, so a session inheriting that setting wrote files outside the working folder
   860	    // without asking once. The gateway is not the protocol but this setting.
   861	    let app_data = app
   862	        .path()
   863	        .app_data_dir()
   864	        .map_err(|err| format!("app-data-dir-unavailable:{err}"))?;
   865	    // Starting without a verified app-owned boundary is never allowed. A launcher that lacks
   866	    // isolation or fails its preparation is a start failure; launching in that state can inherit
   867	    // the user's global allow list while the screen has no enforceable gateway.
   868	    // Shadow walking requires asking "is this item still valid," which needs the CLI's **absolute
   869	    // path**. Launching by name depends on the PATH visible to children, but a GUI app's default PATH differs from the user's shell (measurement at the top of this file).
   870	    let isolation_cli = acp::registry_agent(&runtime_id)
   871	        .and_then(|agent| agent.cli.as_deref())
   872	        .and_then(|name| {
   873	            let dirs = acp::candidate_bin_dirs(
   874	                home.as_deref(),
   875	                std::env::var_os("PATH").as_deref(),
   876	                &probe,
   877	                managed_bin.as_deref(),
   878	                managed_node_bin.as_deref(),
   879	            );
   880	            acp::resolve_command(name, &dirs, &probe)
   881	        });
   882	    let (isolation_env, isolation_dir) = acp::prepare_runtime_isolation(
   883	        &runtime_id,
   884	        &app_data,
   885	        home.as_deref(),
   886	        isolation_cli.as_deref(),
   887	        &launch.path_env,
   888	    )?;
   889	
   890	    let mut command = Command::new(&launch.program);
   891	    command
   892	        .args(&launch.args)
   893	        .current_dir(&root)
   894	        .stdin(Stdio::piped())
   895	        .stdout(Stdio::piped())
   896	        .stderr(Stdio::piped());
   897	    acp::apply_runtime_environment(&mut command, &runtime_id, &launch.path_env);
   898	    command.env(isolation_env, isolation_dir);
   899	
   900	    #[cfg(unix)]
   901	    {
   902	        use std::os::unix::process::CommandExt;
   903	        command.process_group(0);
   904	    }
   905	    #[cfg(windows)]
   906	    {
   907	        // Prevent the child from opening a console window. The app itself has windows_subsystem
   908	        // set, but the child does not inherit it.
   909	        use std::os::windows::process::CommandExt;
   910	        const CREATE_NO_WINDOW: u32 = 0x0800_0000;
   911	        command.creation_flags(CREATE_NO_WINDOW);
   912	    }
   913	
   914	    let mut child = command
   915	        .spawn()
   916	        .map_err(|err| format!("spawn-failed:{err}"))?;
   917	    let pid = child.id();
   918	    let seq = ACP_SESSION_SEQ.fetch_add(1, std::sync::atomic::Ordering::Relaxed);
   919	    let session_id = format!("acp-{seq}-{pid}");
   920	
   921	    let stdin = child.stdin.take().ok_or("stdin-unavailable")?;
   922	    let stdout = child.stdout.take().ok_or("stdout-unavailable")?;
   923	    let stderr = child.stderr.take().ok_or("stderr-unavailable")?;
   924	
   925	    spawn_acp_line_pump(app.clone(), session_id.clone(), stdout, "acp://message");
   926	    spawn_acp_line_pump(app.clone(), session_id.clone(), stderr, "acp://stderr");
   927	
   928	    // ⚠️ **Registration must come first** (caught in 2026-08-16 review).
   929	    //
   930	    // The thread below removes the child from the registry after it ends. Previously, we launched that thread first

hook: PostToolUse
